REVISION 4.0 — INSTITUTION EXTENSION
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Purpose: Add institution accounting and campus tree assessment to the same app.

Institution entry:
  LoginScreen -> choose Institution -> existing Firebase phone OTP -> server profile.

Reuse:
  Existing authentication, API transport, theme, UI controls, AR, GPS, species
  detection and shared satellite/biomass utilities through institution adapters.

Separate records:
  Institution profiles, memberships, campuses, surveys, annual audits and advice.
  Farmer KYC, land, wallet, audits, credits and production flow remain unchanged.

Document responsibilities:
  SRS   : Requirements and acceptance.
  BSDD  : Database, APIs, formulas, server jobs and environment configuration.
  FDD   : Screens, components, state and storage.
  FFC   : Navigation, actions, return paths and complete journeys.
  Demo  : Test accounts, checkpoint content, setup and reset behavior.

Reading rule:
  Keep the existing farmer sections as the baseline. Use the institution chapters
  below for the upgrade; the older upgrade proposal is background material.

Status:
  Specifications are implementation contracts. Live deployment/device readiness
  still requires SRS 35–36 evidence. This edit changes documentation only.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHAT CHANGED FROM PREVIOUS VERSION (v2.0)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

1. Self-hosted PostgreSQL replaced by Supabase (cloud-hosted, free tier).
  Reason: No server needed. Supabase provides PostgreSQL + PostGIS + file
  storage in one platform. All PostGIS spatial queries work identically.

2. Firebase Auth restored as the dedicated identity provider.
  Reason: Phone OTP is handled natively in Firebase Authentication on Android,
  while Supabase remains focused on PostgreSQL/PostGIS + Storage.

3. Satellite fusion engine completely rebuilt.
   Old: Single Sentinel-1 VH band only. Single k-factor linear calibration.
   New: 6-layer data stack — NISAR L-band (primary, from June 2026),
   Sentinel-1 C-band (supplementary), Sentinel-2 optical (NDVI, EVI,
   Red-Edge), GEDI canopy height, SRTM topography. XGBoost model replaces
   single k-factor. Speckle filtering added to all SAR data.
   Expected error: Reduced from ~35% to ~5-7%.

4. Tree height source changed.
   Old: AR measurement from farmer (phone anchor method).
   New: NASA GEDI rh98 canopy height from space. AR height used as fallback
  only for zones where GET /api/v1/audit/zones marks gedi_available=false.

5. Sampling zones are satellite-guided.
   Old: Fixed arbitrary zones (N, S, Centre).
   New: Backend runs Sentinel-2 NDVI analysis to identify distinct vegetation
   density zones. Zones placed inside high, medium, low density areas.

6. Pinata is the chosen IPFS provider.
  Reason: The backend only needs standards-compliant IPFS pinning for audit
  metadata plus a dedicated gateway domain for certificate URLs.

7. Smart contract fixed.
  Fixed: creditAmount multiplication bug (removed * 10**18 scaling).
       Contract now accepts integer deci-CTT units.
   Added: retireCredits() burn function for carbon market compliance.
   Added: Double-mint prevention mapping (auditMinted).
   Added: Hardhat config for Polygon Amoy testnet.

8. Planet Labs NICFI data removed.
   Reason: No response received from Planet Labs. Not included in project.

9. S3 file storage removed.
   Reason: Supabase Storage (1GB free) replaces S3 for evidence photos.

10. OCR engine changed from Tesseract to Google Cloud Vision API.
  Reason: Cloud Vision handles multilingual structured documents without
  bundling local model weights or OCR binaries into the backend image.
  OpenCV remains responsible only for image cleanup and normalization before
  OCR, and the same backend Google service account can be reused for Vision,
  Firebase Admin, and Earth Engine via Application Default Credentials.

================================================================================
SECTION 1: PREREQUISITES AND ACCOUNTS SETUP
================================================================================

Before writing a single line of backend code, all external accounts and keys
must be set up. This section defines every account, what it provides, and
exactly how to set it up.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1.1 FIREBASE AUTHENTICATION (Phone OTP)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

What it provides:
  - Phone number OTP authentication for the mobile app
  - Firebase ID tokens for backend authentication
  - Test phone numbers and fixed OTP codes for QA and emulator testing
  - Firebase Admin SDK verification on the FastAPI backend

Setup steps:
  Step 1: Go to console.firebase.google.com. Create a project named
          terratrust-ar. Use the same underlying Google Cloud project you plan
          to use for Maps and Earth Engine.
  Step 2: Add the Android app. Download google-services.json and place it in
          android/app/ of the mobile project.
  Step 3: Register BOTH SHA-1 and SHA-256 signing fingerprints for the app.
          Firebase Phone Auth relies on app verification (Play Integrity when
          available, reCAPTCHA fallback otherwise).
  Step 4: Go to Authentication → Sign-in method. Enable the Phone provider.
          Set the SMS region policy to allow India (+91) and any testing
          regions you need.
  Step 5: Add fictional phone numbers and fixed OTP codes in the Firebase
          Console for development and QA.
    Step 6: Enable Firebase App Check for the Android app.
      Development builds use the Debug provider.
      Production builds use Play Integrity.
      Enforce App Check before production rollout to reduce OTP abuse.
    Step 7: Do NOT generate a separate Firebase-only JSON key for this
      project.
      TerraTrust uses one shared backend Google service account created
      in Google Cloud setup below. Firebase Admin SDK reads that
      credential through GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS.

Environment variables from this step:
  FIREBASE_PROJECT_ID=[your-firebase-project-id]

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1.2 SUPABASE (Database + File Storage)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

What it provides:
  - PostgreSQL 15 with PostGIS extension (all spatial queries work identically)
  - File storage for land documents and evidence photos
  - Visual dashboard to inspect tables, storage, and logs

Important architecture note:
  Supabase Auth is NOT used in this design. The mobile app does not connect to
  Supabase directly. All database and storage access goes through FastAPI after
  Firebase ID token verification.

Free tier limits (as of March 2026):
  - 500MB database storage
  - 1GB file storage
  - 5GB egress
  - Free projects pause after 1 week of inactivity

Setup steps:
  Step 1: Go to supabase.com. Click "Start your project". Sign up.
  Step 2: Click "New Project". Name it "terratrust-ar". Choose region:
          Southeast Asia (Singapore) — closest free region to India.
  Step 3: Set a strong database password. Save it. Click "Create new project".
  Step 4: Wait 2 minutes for project to initialise.
  Step 5: Go to SQL Editor in dashboard. Run:
            CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS postgis;
            CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS postgis_topology;
          Click "Run". Confirm "Success".
  Step 6: Go to Settings → API. Copy and save:
            - Project URL: https://[ref].supabase.co
            - service_role/secret key (backend only — never expose this)
  Step 7: Create the private storage buckets used by the backend:
            - land-documents
            - evidence-photos

Environment variables from this step:
  SUPABASE_URL=https://[your-ref].supabase.co
  SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY=[service_role_key]   # Backend only, never in app

Keep-alive ping to prevent 1-week pause:
  Add a cron job at cron-job.org (free) to hit your FastAPI health endpoint
  every 5 days:
    URL: https://[your-api-domain]/health
    Method: GET
    Schedule: Every 5 days

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1.3 GOOGLE EARTH ENGINE (Satellite Data)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

What it provides:
  - Access to entire Sentinel-1, Sentinel-2, GEDI, SRTM datasets
  - Server-side processing — no data download needed
  - Python API (earthengine-api) for all satellite operations
  - Free for non-commercial and research use
  - Petabytes of satellite imagery available

Setup steps:
  Step 1: Go to signup.earthengine.google.com.
          Sign in with your Google account.
          Select "Academic or research use" category.
          Fill in project description: "Carbon credit verification for
          smallholder agroforestry farmers in India using multi-modal satellite
          fusion."
          Submit. Approval usually takes 1-3 days.
  Step 2: Once approved, go to console.cloud.google.com.
          Select your project (same one used for Maps SDK).
          Go to APIs & Services → Library.
          Search "Earth Engine API". Click Enable.
    Step 3: Create one shared backend service account for server-side Google
      integrations:
          APIs & Services → Credentials → Create Credentials
          → Service Account.
      Name: "terratrust-backend-service"
          Role: "Earth Engine" → "Earth Engine Resource Writer"
          Click Done.
  Step 4: Click on the service account just created.
          Go to Keys tab → Add Key → Create new key → JSON.
      Download the JSON file. Save as: backend-google-service-account.json
          NEVER commit this file to git. Add to .gitignore.
    Step 5: Ensure the Cloud project itself is registered for Earth Engine:
      Go to console.cloud.google.com/earth-engine.
      Confirm the project is registered and the Earth Engine API is enabled.
      IMPORTANT: Earth Engine now applies noncommercial quota tiers at the
      project level. Select the correct tier for this project before
      rollout. If no tier is selected by April 27, 2026, the project will
      use the Community Tier by default.
    Step 6: Enable Cloud Vision API in the same Google Cloud project and reuse
      this same backend service account for Vision, Firebase Admin, and Earth
      Engine. Do not create separate JSON keys per Google service.

Environment variables from this step:
  GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT=[google-cloud-project-id]
  GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS=./backend-google-service-account.json

Test command (run after setup):
  import ee
  ee.Initialize(project='[google-cloud-project-id]')
  print(ee.Number(42).getInfo())  # Should print 42

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1.4 NISAR SATELLITE DATA (Alaska Satellite Facility)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

What it provides:
  - NISAR L-band SAR data (launched July 2025, operational January 2026)
  - Free global L-band radar data, 1-2 days after acquisition
  - Data hosted on AWS, accessible via asf_search Python library
  - Covers India every 6-12 days
  - Promotion to production is expected only after calibration is confirmed

IMPORTANT PHASING:
  Before June 2026: Use Sentinel-1 as primary SAR. Use NISAR data for
  development and testing only (pre-calibration data available but not
  production-grade).
  After calibration is confirmed in production readiness testing: switch NISAR
  to primary SAR source. Sentinel-1 becomes supplementary.

Setup steps:
  Step 1: Go to urs.earthdata.nasa.gov. Create a free NASA Earthdata account.
  Step 2: Go to asf.alaska.edu. Sign in with Earthdata credentials.
  Step 3: Accept the End User License Agreement for NISAR data.
  Step 4: In your terminal:
            pip install asf_search
            python -c "import asf_search; asf_search.ASFSession().auth_with_creds(
              'YOUR_USERNAME', 'YOUR_PASSWORD')"
          This saves credentials to ~/.netrc for automated access.

Environment variables from this step:
  NASA_EARTHDATA_USERNAME=[your username]
  NASA_EARTHDATA_PASSWORD=[your password]

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1.5 METAMASK AND POLYGON WALLET (Blockchain)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

What it provides:
  - Admin/Oracle wallet: the blockchain identity of your backend server
  - This wallet is the only one authorised to call mint() on the smart contract
  - POL test tokens needed for gas fees (transaction costs on Polygon Amoy)

Setup steps:
  Step 1: Install MetaMask browser extension at metamask.io.
  Step 2: Create a new wallet. Write down the 12-word seed phrase on paper.
          Store it safely offline. If lost, wallet cannot be recovered.
  Step 3: Add Polygon Amoy Testnet to MetaMask:
      Network Name: Polygon Amoy Testnet
      RPC URL: https://rpc-amoy.polygon.technology/
      Chain ID: 80002
      Currency Symbol: POL
      Block Explorer: https://amoy.polygonscan.com
  Step 4: Get free test POL from Polygon faucet:
      Go to faucet.polygon.technology.
      Connect MetaMask. Request test POL.
      This is fake POL for testing — no real money involved.
  Step 5: Export private key from MetaMask:
      Account Details → Export Private Key → Enter password → Copy key.
      NEVER share this key. NEVER commit to git.
  NOTE: Polygon documentation now labels the gas token as POL. Older
      tutorials may still say MATIC, but TerraTrust v3.1 uses the Amoy
      values above only.

Environment variables from this step:
  ADMIN_WALLET_PRIVATE_KEY=[exported private key]
  ADMIN_WALLET_ADDRESS=[your public wallet address]

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1.6 ALCHEMY (Blockchain RPC Provider)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

What it provides:
  - A gateway URL to connect your Python backend to the Polygon network
  - Without this you would need to run your own blockchain node (impossible)
  - Free tier: 300 million compute units per month (far more than needed)

Setup steps:
  Step 1: Go to alchemy.com. Sign up with Google or email.
  Step 2: Click "Create new app".
          Name: TerraTrust-AR
          Chain: Polygon
          Network: Amoy (testnet for development)
          Click Create.
  Step 3: Click "API Key" on your new app.
          Copy the HTTPS URL shown.
          Example: https://polygon-amoy.g.alchemy.com/v2/abc123xyz
  Step 4: TerraTrust v3.1 uses only the Amoy app above. Do not configure a
          mainnet app for testing or pilot use.

Environment variables from this step:
  ALCHEMY_POLYGON_AMOY_URL=https://polygon-amoy.g.alchemy.com/v2/[key]

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1.7 PINATA (IPFS — Metadata Pinning)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

What it provides:
  - IPFS pinning for audit metadata JSON
  - JWT-based backend authentication for pinning requests
  - Dedicated gateway domain for user-facing certificate URLs
  - Standard IPFS CID output for on-chain storage

Setup steps:
  Step 1: Go to app.pinata.cloud. Create an account.
  Step 2: Open API Keys. Click "New Key".
          Start with Admin privileges in development.
          Copy the JWT immediately.
  Step 3: Open Gateways. Copy your gateway domain.
          Example: example-gateway.mypinata.cloud
  Step 4: Backend endpoint used in this project:
          https://api.pinata.cloud/pinning/pinJSONToIPFS

Environment variables from this step:
  PINATA_JWT=[backend jwt]
  PINATA_GATEWAY_URL=[your-gateway].mypinata.cloud

1.8 HARDHAT (Smart Contract Development Tool)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

What it provides:
  - Local blockchain for testing contracts before deploying
  - Deployment scripts for Polygon Amoy testnet
  - Contract verification on PolygonScan
  - Replaces the need for Remix IDE

Setup steps:
  Step 1: Create a contracts/ folder in your project root.
  Step 2: In that folder:
            npm init -y
            npm install --save-dev hardhat
            npm install --save-dev @nomicfoundation/hardhat-toolbox
            npm install @openzeppelin/contracts
            npx hardhat --init
          Select "Create a JavaScript project" when prompted.
  Step 3: Create hardhat.config.js with Polygon Amoy configuration
          (full config provided in Section 7 of this document).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1.9 POLYGONSCAN API (Contract Verification)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

What it provides:
  - Allows you to verify your contract source code publicly on PolygonScan
  - Carbon market auditors can read exactly what your contract does
  - Proves the contract is not hiding any malicious logic
  - Free

Setup steps:
  Step 1: Go to polygonscan.com. Click "Sign In" → "Register".
  Step 2: After registering, go to API Keys section.
          Click "Add". Name: "terratrust". Copy the key.

Environment variables from this step:
  POLYGONSCAN_API_KEY=[your api key]

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1.10 MAHARASHTRA LGD API (Land Records)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

What it provides:
  - Official government REST API for Maharashtra location codes
  - Returns District codes, Taluka codes, Village codes in JSON
  - These codes are used to construct the gis_code for BhuNaksha WMS
  - Officially published by Maharashtra government at mahavillages.mahabhumi.gov.in
  - No authentication required, no API key needed

Base URL: http://115.124.105.220/API/

No setup required. No account needed. Call directly from backend.

Key endpoints used:
  POST /GetAllDistricts                   Returns all district codes
  POST /GetTalukasOfDistrict              Input: distcode → Returns taluka codes
  POST /GetVillagesOfDistrictAndTaluka    Input: distcode + talukacode → village codes

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1.11 SUMMARY OF ALL ENVIRONMENT VARIABLES
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Create a file named .env in your backend project root.
NEVER commit this file to git. Add .env to .gitignore immediately.

  # Firebase Authentication
  FIREBASE_PROJECT_ID=[firebase-project-id]

  # Supabase
  SUPABASE_URL=https://[ref].supabase.co
  SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY=[service_role_key]

  # Shared Google backend credentials
  GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT=[google-cloud-project-id]
  GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS=./backend-google-service-account.json

  # NASA NISAR
  NASA_EARTHDATA_USERNAME=[username]
  NASA_EARTHDATA_PASSWORD=[password]
  NISAR_PRODUCTION_READY=false

  # Blockchain
  ADMIN_WALLET_PRIVATE_KEY=[private key]
  ADMIN_WALLET_ADDRESS=[public address]
  ALCHEMY_POLYGON_AMOY_URL=https://polygon-amoy.g.alchemy.com/v2/[key]
  CONTRACT_ADDRESS=[deployed contract address — filled after Section 7]

  # IPFS
  PINATA_JWT=[jwt]
  PINATA_GATEWAY_URL=[your-gateway].mypinata.cloud

  # PolygonScan
  POLYGONSCAN_API_KEY=[key]

  # Government APIs
  LGD_API_BASE=http://115.124.105.220/API  # Maharashtra LGD API base URL

  # App Config
  ENVIRONMENT=development        # or production
  REDIS_URL=redis://localhost:6379/0   # Update to cloud Redis URL for production
  WEB_CORS_ORIGINS=https://[future-web-domain]   # Optional. Not required for mobile-only RN clients.

================================================================================
SECTION 2: ARCHITECTURE OVERVIEW

v4.0 architecture extension: shared services below support two separate domain
adapters. Institution architecture/auth boundaries are authoritative in Section 15.
================================================================================

2.1 DESIGN PATTERN
------------------
The backend follows a Modular Monolithic Architecture, designed to be
migratable to Microservices when the project scales. It uses an Asynchronous
Event-Driven model for long-running operations.

What "modular monolithic" means in practice: all services (Auth, Land, Fusion,
Blockchain) live in one Python project and one deployment. But they are
structured as separate modules with clean interfaces between them, so any
single module can be extracted to its own service later without rewriting.

What "async event-driven" means: when a farmer submits their audit data, the
API immediately returns a response ("processing started"). The heavy satellite
computation runs in the background via Celery tasks executed by a worker
process running alongside FastAPI in the same backend service. The mobile app
polls for results. This prevents the API from timing out during 60-second
satellite jobs.

2.2 TECHNOLOGY STACK
---------------------

Component          | Technology              | Version | Reason
-------------------|-------------------------|---------|---------------------------
Runtime            | Python                  | 3.11+   | Mature geospatial,
                   |                         |         | AI (scikit-learn/XGBoost),
                   |                         |         | and async support.
API Framework      | FastAPI                 | 0.110+  | Async I/O for high
                   |                         |         | concurrency. Auto-generates
                   |                         |         | Swagger docs. Type-safe.
ASGI Server        | Uvicorn                 | Latest  | Production ASGI server
                   |                         |         | for FastAPI.
Database           | Supabase                | Managed | PostgreSQL 15 + PostGIS
                   |                         |         | 3.3 in cloud. Free tier.
                   |                         |         | No server management.
Authentication     | Firebase Authentication | Managed | Phone OTP for the mobile app.
                   |                         |         | Backend verifies Firebase
                   |                         |         | ID tokens with Admin SDK.
Database Client    | supabase-py             | Latest  | Python client for Supabase.
ORM / Queries      | SQLAlchemy + asyncpg    | 2.0+    | Async PostgreSQL queries.
                   |                         |         | Raw SQL for PostGIS spatial
                   |                         |         | operations.
Task Queue         | Celery                  | 5.3+    | Background processing for
                   |                         |         | satellite computation and
                   |                         |         | blockchain transactions.
Message Broker     | Redis                   | 7.0+    | Celery message broker.
                   |                         |         | Use Upstash Redis over TLS
                   |                         |         | via REDIS_URL.
Satellite Engine   | Google Earth Engine API | Latest  | Server-side access to
                   |                         |         | Sentinel-1, Sentinel-2,
                   |                         |         | GEDI, SRTM datasets.
NISAR Access       | asf_search              | Latest  | Python library to search
                   |                         |         | and download NISAR L-band
                   |                         |         | data from Alaska Satellite
                   |                         |         | Facility.
ML Model           | XGBoost                 | 2.0+    | Gradient boosted trees for
                   |                         |         | multi-feature biomass
                   |                         |         | regression. Replaces single
                   |                         |         | k-factor approach.
OCR Engine         | Google Cloud Vision API | Latest  | OCR for 7/12 land document
                   | + OpenCV                |         | field extraction. Uses
                   |                         |         | DOCUMENT_TEXT_DETECTION and
                   |                         |         | shared backend Google creds.
Blockchain Client  | web3.py                 | 7.x     | Python interface to call
                   |                         |         | Polygon smart contract
                   |                         |         | mint() and retire()
                   |                         |         | functions.
IPFS Upload        | Pinata Pinning API      | REST    | Pin audit metadata JSON to
                   |                         |         | IPFS and expose it through
                   |                         |         | a dedicated gateway domain.
Validation         | Pydantic                | 2.0+    | Request/response data
                   |                         |         | validation. Built into
                   |                         |         | FastAPI.
Spatial Library    | Shapely                 | 2.0+    | GeoJSON polygon validation
                   |                         |         | and geometry operations.
Image Processing   | OpenCV (cv2)            | 4.9+    | Document image processing
                   |                         |         | for Layer 3 manual upload.

2.3 SYSTEM FLOW OVERVIEW
--------------------------

The backend sits between the mobile app and three external systems:

Mobile App
    |
    | HTTPS + JWT (Firebase ID token)
    v
FastAPI Backend
    |
    |--- Land Service -------> Maharashtra LGD API (get location codes)
    |                    ----> BhuNaksha WMS (get official boundary GeoJSON)
    |                    ----> Supabase PostGIS (store verified boundary)
    |
    |--- Fusion Service -----> Google Earth Engine (satellite imagery)
    |                    ----> Alaska Satellite Facility (NISAR data)
    |                    ----> Supabase (store biomass audit results)
    |
    |--- Minting Service ----> Pinata IPFS (pin metadata)
                         ----> Polygon Blockchain via Alchemy (mint tokens)
                         ----> Supabase (store tx_hash)

================================================================================
SECTION 4: DATABASE DESIGN (SUPABASE + POSTGIS)

v4.0: preserved farmer schema below; new domain/tenant tables and invariants in
Section 16 are additive and never repurpose farmer audit or land records.
================================================================================

4.1 SETUP
---------
Run these SQL commands in Supabase SQL Editor before anything else:

  -- Enable PostGIS extensions
  CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS postgis;
  CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS postgis_topology;
  CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS "uuid-ossp";

  -- Verify PostGIS is working
  SELECT PostGIS_Version();

4.2 TABLE: users
-----------------
Purpose: Stores farmer identity, authentication linkage, and blockchain wallet.

  CREATE TABLE users (
      id               UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT uuid_generate_v4(),
      firebase_uid     VARCHAR(128) UNIQUE NOT NULL,
      phone_number     VARCHAR(15) UNIQUE NOT NULL,
      full_name        VARCHAR(255),
      aadhaar_hash     VARCHAR(64),       -- SHA-256 hash, never plain text
      wallet_address   VARCHAR(42),       -- Polygon public address (0x...)
      role             VARCHAR(20) DEFAULT 'FARMER',  -- FARMER, AUDITOR, ADMIN
      kyc_completed    BOOLEAN DEFAULT FALSE,
      created_at       TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE DEFAULT NOW(),
      updated_at       TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE DEFAULT NOW()
  );

  CREATE INDEX idx_users_firebase_uid ON users(firebase_uid);
  CREATE INDEX idx_users_phone ON users(phone_number);
  CREATE INDEX idx_users_wallet ON users(wallet_address);

4.2A TABLE: wallet_recovery_requests
------------------------------------
Purpose: Stores admin-assisted wallet rotation requests initiated from the
mobile app. Historical audit evidence and already-issued on-chain token
ownership remain tied to the original wallet; approved requests affect only
future credit issuance and future certificates.

  CREATE TABLE wallet_recovery_requests (
      id                 UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT uuid_generate_v4(),
      user_id            UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES users(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
      old_wallet_address VARCHAR(42),
      new_wallet_address VARCHAR(42) NOT NULL,
      status             VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'PENDING',
                         -- 'PENDING', 'APPROVED', 'REJECTED', 'COMPLETED'
      requested_at       TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE DEFAULT NOW(),
      reviewed_at        TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE,
      completed_at       TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE,
      reviewer_note      TEXT
  );

  CREATE INDEX idx_wallet_recovery_user ON wallet_recovery_requests(user_id);
  CREATE INDEX idx_wallet_recovery_status ON wallet_recovery_requests(status);

4.3 TABLE: land_parcels
-----------------------
Purpose: Stores verified farm land boundaries as spatial geometry.
This is the most critical table — every biomass calculation is clipped to
this polygon.

  CREATE TABLE land_parcels (
      id               UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT uuid_generate_v4(),
      user_id          UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES users(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
      farm_name        VARCHAR(255),      -- User-given editable name e.g. "North Field"
      survey_number    VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL,   -- Extracted by OCR from 7/12
      district         VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
      taluka           VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
      village          VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
      state            VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'Maharashtra',
      geom             GEOMETRY(POLYGON, 4326) NOT NULL,  -- Official boundary
      area_hectares    FLOAT,             -- Auto-calculated by PostGIS
      is_verified      BOOLEAN DEFAULT FALSE,
      boundary_source  VARCHAR(50),       -- 'WMS_AUTO', 'SCRAPE', 'MANUAL'
      ocr_owner_name   VARCHAR(255),      -- Name extracted from 7/12 document
      doc_image_url    VARCHAR(500),      -- Supabase Storage URL of 7/12 scan
      lgd_district_code VARCHAR(10),      -- From LGD API
      lgd_taluka_code   VARCHAR(10),
      lgd_village_code  VARCHAR(10),
      gis_code         VARCHAR(50),       -- Constructed for BhuNaksha WMS
        created_at       TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE DEFAULT NOW(),
        updated_at       TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE DEFAULT NOW()
  );

  -- GIST spatial index — required for all PostGIS geometry queries
  CREATE INDEX idx_land_parcels_geom ON land_parcels USING GIST(geom);
  CREATE INDEX idx_land_parcels_user ON land_parcels(user_id);

  -- Trigger: auto-calculate area_hectares whenever geom is inserted/updated
  CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION update_land_area()
  RETURNS TRIGGER AS $$
  BEGIN
      NEW.updated_at := NOW();
      NEW.area_hectares := ST_Area(NEW.geom::geography) / 10000;
      RETURN NEW;
  END;
  $$ LANGUAGE plpgsql;

  CREATE TRIGGER trg_land_area
  BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE ON land_parcels
  FOR EACH ROW EXECUTE FUNCTION update_land_area();

4.4 TABLE: sampling_zones
--------------------------
Purpose: Stores satellite-guided sampling zones generated per audit session.

  CREATE TABLE sampling_zones (
      id               UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT uuid_generate_v4(),
      land_id          UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES land_parcels(id),
      audit_id         UUID,              -- Linked when audit starts
      zone_label       VARCHAR(5) NOT NULL,  -- 'A', 'B', 'C', etc.
      centre_point     GEOMETRY(POINT, 4326) NOT NULL,  -- GPS to walk to
      radius_metres    FLOAT NOT NULL,    -- Scan all trees within this radius
      zone_type        VARCHAR(20),       -- 'high_density', 'medium_density', 'low_density'
      ndvi_mean        FLOAT,             -- Average NDVI in this zone
      gedi_available   BOOLEAN DEFAULT FALSE,
      sequence_order   INT NOT NULL,      -- Order in walking path
      created_at       TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE DEFAULT NOW()
  );

  CREATE INDEX idx_zones_land ON sampling_zones(land_id);
  CREATE INDEX idx_zones_audit ON sampling_zones(audit_id);

4.5 TABLE: ar_tree_scans
-------------------------
Purpose: Stores individual tree measurements from AR scanning session.
These are the ground truth data points used to train the XGBoost model.

  CREATE TABLE ar_tree_scans (
      id               UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT uuid_generate_v4(),
      audit_id         UUID NOT NULL,
      land_id          UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES land_parcels(id),
      zone_id          UUID REFERENCES sampling_zones(id),
      gps_location     GEOMETRY(POINT, 4326) NOT NULL,
      gps_accuracy_m   FLOAT,             -- GPS horizontal accuracy (m)
      species          VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,  -- TF Lite detected species
      species_confidence FLOAT CHECK (species_confidence BETWEEN 0 AND 1),
      species_source   VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL,   -- MODEL_AUTO / MODEL_CONFIRMED / MANUAL_SELECTED
      dbh_cm           FLOAT NOT NULL CHECK (dbh_cm > 0 AND dbh_cm < 500),
      height_m         FLOAT,             -- AR-measured height (fallback only)
      gedi_height_m    FLOAT,             -- GEDI rh98 height resolved for this tree/zone
      height_source    VARCHAR(20),       -- 'GEDI' or 'AR_FALLBACK'
      wood_density     FLOAT NOT NULL,    -- Species-specific rho value (g/cm3)
      agb_kg           FLOAT,             -- Calculated by backend via Chave eq
      ar_tier_used     INT CHECK (ar_tier_used IN (1, 2, 3)),
      confidence_score FLOAT CHECK (confidence_score BETWEEN 0 AND 1),
      evidence_photo_path VARCHAR(500),   -- Supabase Storage object path
      evidence_photo_hash VARCHAR(64),    -- SHA-256 hash of photo
      scan_timestamp   TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE NOT NULL,
      created_at       TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE DEFAULT NOW()
  );

  CREATE INDEX idx_scans_audit ON ar_tree_scans(audit_id);
  CREATE INDEX idx_scans_land ON ar_tree_scans(land_id);
  CREATE INDEX idx_scans_gps ON ar_tree_scans USING GIST(gps_location);

4.6 TABLE: carbon_audits
-------------------------
Purpose: Stores the final verified carbon calculation for each land per year.
This is the permanent ledger of all carbon credit generations.

  CREATE TABLE carbon_audits (
      id               UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT uuid_generate_v4(),
      land_id          UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES land_parcels(id),
      user_id          UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES users(id),
      audit_year       INT NOT NULL,
      status           VARCHAR(20) DEFAULT 'PROCESSING',
      -- Status values: PROCESSING, CALCULATING, READY_TO_MINT,
      --                MINTED, COMPLETE_NO_CREDITS, FAILED
      -- Satellite data used
      s1_vh_mean_db    FLOAT,             -- Sentinel-1 VH mean backscatter
      s1_vv_mean_db    FLOAT,             -- Sentinel-1 VV mean backscatter
      s2_ndvi_mean     FLOAT,             -- Sentinel-2 NDVI mean
      s2_evi_mean      FLOAT,             -- Sentinel-2 EVI mean
      gedi_height_mean FLOAT,             -- GEDI rh98 mean over land
      srtm_elevation_mean FLOAT,          -- SRTM mean elevation
      nisar_used       BOOLEAN DEFAULT FALSE,  -- Whether NISAR data was used
      -- Model results
      xgboost_model_version VARCHAR(20),  -- Model version used
      features_count   INT,               -- Normally 9 or 12; 8 or 11 when GEDI is unavailable
      total_biomass_tonnes FLOAT,         -- Total AGB for entire land
      -- Carbon calculation
      prev_year_biomass FLOAT,            -- From previous audit
      delta_biomass    FLOAT,             -- Growth = current - previous
      carbon_tonnes    FLOAT,             -- delta * 0.47
      co2_equivalent   FLOAT,             -- carbon * 3.667 (44/12 = CO2 conversion)
      credits_issued   FLOAT,             -- Human-readable CTT amount
                  -- On-chain raw units = credits_issued * 10
      trees_scanned_count INT,
      -- Blockchain
      ipfs_metadata_cid VARCHAR(255),     -- IPFS CID of evidence package
      tx_hash          VARCHAR(66),       -- Polygon transaction hash (0x...)
      token_id         BIGINT,            -- ERC-1155 audit certificate ID
      minted_at        TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE,
      created_at       TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE DEFAULT NOW()
  );

  CREATE UNIQUE INDEX idx_audits_land_year ON carbon_audits(land_id, audit_year);
  CREATE INDEX idx_audits_user ON carbon_audits(user_id);
  CREATE INDEX idx_audits_status ON carbon_audits(status);

4.7 IMPORTANT POSTGIS QUERIES
------------------------------

-- Check if a GPS point is inside a land boundary (geofencing)
SELECT id FROM land_parcels
WHERE user_id = '[user_uuid]'
AND ST_Contains(geom, ST_SetSRID(ST_MakePoint([lng], [lat]), 4326));

-- Get area of a land parcel in hectares
SELECT ST_Area(geom::geography) / 10000 AS area_hectares
FROM land_parcels WHERE id = '[land_uuid]';

-- Get last year's biomass for delta calculation
SELECT total_biomass_tonnes FROM carbon_audits
WHERE land_id = '[land_uuid]'
ORDER BY audit_year DESC LIMIT 1;

-- Validate that a submitted GeoJSON polygon is valid geometry
SELECT ST_IsValid(
    ST_GeomFromGeoJSON('[geojson_string]')
) AS is_valid;

================================================================================
SECTION 5: API SPECIFICATION (FASTAPI)

v4.0: preserve farmer shapes. Compatible /auth/me domain provisioning is defined
in 15.2; new institution namespace/review endpoints are defined in Section 18.
================================================================================

Base URL (development): http://localhost:8000
Base URL (production):  https://[your-domain]

All endpoints (except /health and /api/v1/status) require:
Authorization: Bearer [firebase_id_token]
The token is returned by Firebase Authentication after OTP verification.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
5.1 HEALTH CHECK
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

GET /health
  Purpose:   Confirms backend is running. Used by keep-alive cron job.
  Auth:      None required
  Response:  { "status": "ok", "timestamp": "2026-03-07T10:00:00Z" }

GET /api/v1/status
  Purpose:   Farmer-facing app status check used during app bootstrap before
             the authenticated profile flow begins.
  Auth:      None required
  Response:  { "maintenance": false, "message": null }
             or
             { "maintenance": true,
               "message": "Scheduled maintenance in progress" }
  Note:      When maintenance mode is active, any farmer-facing endpoint may
             also short-circuit with HTTP 503 and the same JSON payload.
             While the maintenance screen is visible, the mobile app polls
             GET /api/v1/status every 10 seconds until maintenance=false.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
5.2 AUTH MODULE (/api/v1/auth)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

POST /api/v1/auth/kyc
  Purpose:   Saves farmer KYC identity data after first OTP login.
  Auth:      Required (Firebase ID token)
  Request:   {
               "full_name": "Ramesh Patil",
               "aadhaar_number": "XXXXXXXXXXXX"   // 12 digits, will be hashed
             }
  Reason:    OTP verifies control of the phone number, but not the claimant's
             real-world identity. Aadhaar is the v3.1 KYC identifier used to
             anchor the farmer record, support owner-name matching against the
             7/12 extract, and re-verify the same person during wallet recovery.
  Process:   1. Hash aadhaar_number with SHA-256. Store hash only.
             2. Store full_name in users table.
             3. Set kyc_completed = true.
  Response:  { "status": "success", "user_id": "uuid" }
  Error 400: { "error": "Invalid Aadhaar number format" }

POST /api/v1/auth/register-wallet
  Purpose:   Stores the farmer's public blockchain wallet address after the
             mobile app silently generates it on first login.
  Auth:      Required (Firebase ID token)
  Request:   { "wallet_address": "0x..." }   // Public address only
  Process:   1. Validate wallet_address format (42-char 0x hex string).
             2. Store wallet_address in users table for the authenticated user.
  Response:  { "status": "success" }
  Error 400: { "error": "Invalid wallet address format" }

POST /api/v1/auth/recover-wallet
  Purpose:   Creates an admin-assisted wallet rotation request after the
         farmer re-verifies identity on a new device.
  Auth:      Required (Firebase ID token)
  Request:   { "new_wallet_address": "0x..." }
  Process:   1. Verify the user has completed KYC and already has an
           existing wallet on record.
         2. Validate new_wallet_address format and ensure it differs from
           the current wallet.
         3. Insert a PENDING row into wallet_recovery_requests.
         4. Return a pending response. Do not swap wallet_address yet;
           approval is required before future issuance changes.
  Response:  { "status": "pending",
          "message": "Wallet recovery request submitted" }
  Error 400: { "error": "Invalid wallet address format" }
  Error 409: { "error": "A wallet recovery request is already pending" }

GET /api/v1/auth/me
  Purpose:   Bootstrap TerraTrust profile state after Firebase sign-in.
  Auth:      Required (Firebase ID token)
  Process:   1. Verify Firebase ID token.
             2. Extract uid + phone_number.
             3. Upsert minimal users row if missing.
             4. Return KYC and wallet status.
  Response:  {
               "user_id": "uuid",
               "firebase_uid": "firebase-uid",
               "phone_number": "+91XXXXXXXXXX",
               "full_name": "Ramesh Patil",
               "kyc_completed": true,
               "wallet_address": "0x...",
               "wallet_recovery_status": null,
               "wallet_recovery_requested_at": null
             }

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
5.3 LAND MODULE (/api/v1/land)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

POST /api/v1/land/verify-document
  Purpose:   Accepts 7/12 document photo. Runs OCR. Returns extracted fields.
  Auth:      Required
  Request:   multipart/form-data
             image: [photo file — JPG or PNG, max 10MB]
  Process:
    1. Receive image. Save temporarily.
    2. Pre-process with OpenCV:
         - Normalize orientation from EXIF metadata when available
         - Convert to greyscale
         - Apply adaptive thresholding (binarise)
         - Denoise
         - Optionally retry with original image bytes if preprocessing hurts OCR
    3. Run Google Cloud Vision DOCUMENT_TEXT_DETECTION:
         from google.cloud import vision
         client = vision.ImageAnnotatorClient()
         response = client.document_text_detection(
             image=vision.Image(content=processed_img_bytes)
         )
         text = response.full_text_annotation.text
    4. Regex extraction:
         survey_number = re.search(r'(?:Survey|Gat|सर्व्हे)\s*[Nn]o\.?\s*:?\s*(\d+)', text)
         owner_name    = re.search(r'(?:Owner|मालक|धारक)\s*:?\s*([A-Z\u0900-\u097F\s]+)', text)
         village       = re.search(r'(?:Village|गाव)\s*:?\s*([A-Z\u0900-\u097F\s]+)', text)
         taluka        = re.search(r'(?:Taluka|तालुका)\s*:?\s*([A-Z\u0900-\u097F\s]+)', text)
         district      = re.search(r'(?:District|जिल्हा)\s*:?\s*([A-Z\u0900-\u097F\s]+)', text)
    5. Return extracted fields. Do NOT cross-check owner name here — that
       happens in the next step when farmer confirms.
  Response:  {
               "survey_number": "47",
               "owner_name": "Ramesh Shankar Patil",
               "village": "Kharadi",
               "taluka": "Haveli",
               "district": "Pune",
               "state": "Maharashtra",
               "extraction_confidence": 0.87   // 0.0 to 1.0
             }
  Error 422: { "error": "Could not extract required fields. Image quality too
                          low. Please retake photo." }

GET /api/v1/land/fetch-boundary
  Purpose:   Auto-fetches official land boundary from government GIS system.
  Auth:      Required
  Query params:
             survey_number=[string]
             district=[string]
             taluka=[string]
             village=[string]
             state=[string]
             user_lat=[float]     // Farmer's current GPS for WMS bbox
             user_lng=[float]
  Process (3-Layer State Router):

    LAYER 1 — LGD API + BhuNaksha WMS (try first):
      a. Call Maharashtra LGD API to get official location codes:
         POST http://115.124.105.220/API/GetTalukasOfDistrict
              body: { "distcode": district_code }
         POST http://115.124.105.220/API/GetVillagesOfDistrictAndTaluka
              body: { "distcode": dist_code, "talukacode": taluka_code }
      b. Construct gis_code:
         Pattern: "RVM" + region_code + division_code + "27" (Maharashtra state)
                  + taluka_lgd_padded_4 + village_lgd_padded_6
                  + survey_number_padded_6
      c. Build WMS bounding box from user GPS (±0.01 degree around farmer loc)
      d. Call BhuNaksha WMS GetFeatureInfo:
         GET https://mahabhunakasha.mahabhumi.gov.in/WMS?
             SERVICE=WMS&VERSION=1.3.0&REQUEST=GetFeatureInfo
             &INFO_FORMAT=application/json
             &QUERY_LAYERS=PLOT_BOUNDARY
             &gis_code=[constructed_code]
             &BBOX=[lng-0.01],[lat-0.01],[lng+0.01],[lat+0.01]
             &WIDTH=800&HEIGHT=600
             &I=[pixel_x]&J=[pixel_y]    // GPS converted to pixel coords
      e. If response is valid GeoJSON polygon: return it. Mark source='WMS_AUTO'

    LAYER 2 — Scraping fallback (if Layer 1 fails):
      a. Use Selenium/Playwright headless browser to navigate BhuNaksha portal
      b. Select District, Taluka, Village from dropdowns using extracted names
      c. Click on survey number to select parcel
      d. Extract polygon coordinates from rendered map canvas
      e. If successful: return GeoJSON. Mark source='SCRAPE'

    LAYER 3 — Manual (if Layers 1 and 2 fail):
      a. Return { "status": "manual_required", "message": "Please download and
                   upload your land map manually" }
      b. Mobile app then shows manual upload guide

  Response:  {
               "status": "success",
               "boundary_source": "WMS_AUTO",
               "satellite_thumbnail_url": "https://[signed-supabase-url]/boundary-47.png",
               "geojson": {
                 "type": "Feature",
                 "geometry": {
                   "type": "Polygon",
                   "coordinates": [[[73.981, 18.545], [73.982, 18.546], ...]]
                 },
                 "properties": {
                   "survey_number": "47",
                   "owner_name": "Ramesh Shankar Patil",
                   "area_sqm": 4856.2
                 }
               }
             }
  Response (fallback): { "status": "manual_required" }

POST /api/v1/land/fetch-boundary
  Purpose:   Layer 3 manual-map processing variant used after the farmer
             uploads a downloaded government parcel map image.
  Auth:      Required
  Request:   multipart/form-data with map_image, survey_number, district,
             taluka, village, state
  Process:   Extract polygon edges from the uploaded map image with OpenCV,
             validate the polygon, generate the same Sentinel-2 thumbnail, and
             return the standard boundary payload with boundary_source='MANUAL'.
  Response:  Same success payload as GET /api/v1/land/fetch-boundary,
             with "boundary_source": "MANUAL".
  Error 422: { "error": "Could not extract a valid parcel boundary from the uploaded map." }

POST /api/v1/land/register
  Purpose:   Saves confirmed land boundary to database after farmer confirms.
  Auth:      Required
  Request:   {
               "farm_name": "North Field",
               "survey_number": "47",
               "district": "Pune",
               "taluka": "Haveli",
               "village": "Kharadi",
               "state": "Maharashtra",
               "boundary_source": "WMS_AUTO",
               "geojson": { ...GeoJSON polygon... },
               "ocr_owner_name": "Ramesh Shankar Patil"
             }
  Process:
    1. Cross-check ocr_owner_name against user's full_name from KYC:
         Use fuzzy string matching (80%+ similarity via difflib or rapidfuzz).
         If similarity is below 80%: Return 400 error.
    2. Validate GeoJSON:
         SELECT ST_IsValid(ST_GeomFromGeoJSON('[geojson]')) — must be TRUE
    3. Check for duplicate: same user_id + survey_number must not already exist
    4. Insert into land_parcels. Area auto-calculated by trigger.
    5. Return land_id.
  Response:  { "land_id": "uuid", "area_hectares": 0.49, "status": "verified" }
  Error 400: { "error": "Owner name on document does not match your registered
                          name." }
  Error 409: { "error": "This survey number is already registered to your
                          account." }

GET /api/v1/land/list
  Purpose:   Returns paginated land parcels registered by the authenticated farmer.
             Called when LandListScreen loads and when HomeScreen fetches the
             parcel preview cards.
  Auth:      Required
  Query:     page=1&limit=20
  Process:   Fetch all land_parcels rows where user_id matches the
             authenticated backend user resolved from the verified Firebase
             ID token.
             Include latest audit year plus current-year audit metadata from
             carbon_audits for each parcel so the app can decide whether to
             show Start Audit, Resume Audit, or View Status.
  Response:  {
               "items": [
                 {
                   "id": "uuid",
                   "farm_name": "North Field",
                   "survey_number": "47",
                   "district": "Pune",
                   "taluka": "Haveli",
                   "village": "Kharadi",
                   "state": "Maharashtra",
                   "area_hectares": 0.49,
                   "is_verified": true,
                   "boundary_source": "WMS_AUTO",
                   "registered_at": "2026-05-18T12:00:00Z",
                   "last_audit_year": 2025,
                    "current_audit_id": "uuid",
                    "current_audit_status": "CALCULATING",
                   "thumbnail_url": "https://[signed-supabase-url]/..."
                 }
               ],
               "page": 1,
               "limit": 20,
               "total": 7,
               "has_more": false
             }

PATCH /api/v1/land/{land_id}
  Purpose:   Updates the farmer-facing parcel name from EditLandNameScreen.
  Auth:      Required
  Request:   { "farm_name": "North Field" }
  Process:   1. Resolve authenticated user from the Firebase ID token.
             2. Verify the land_id belongs to that user.
             3. Update farm_name only. Do not allow geometry or survey edits.
  Response:  { "status": "success", "land_id": "uuid",
               "farm_name": "North Field" }
  Error 404: { "error": "Land parcel not found" }

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
5.4 AUDIT MODULE (/api/v1/audit)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

GET /api/v1/audit/zones
  Purpose:   Generates satellite-guided sampling zones for a land parcel.
             This is the first step of an annual audit.
  Auth:      Required
  Query:     land_id=[uuid]
  Process:
    1. Fetch land boundary GeoJSON from land_parcels table.
    2. Call Google Earth Engine:
         region = ee.Geometry.Polygon(geojson['coordinates'])
         s2 = ee.ImageCollection('COPERNICUS/S2_SR_HARMONIZED')
              .filterBounds(region)
              .filter(ee.Filter.lt('CLOUDY_PIXEL_PERCENTAGE', 20))
              .median()
         ndvi = s2.normalizedDifference(['B8', 'B4'])
         ndvi_values = ndvi.reduceRegion(
             reducer=ee.Reducer.percentile([25, 50, 75]),
             geometry=region, scale=10
         ).getInfo()
    3. Identify zone thresholds from NDVI percentiles:
         low_density:    NDVI < 25th percentile
         medium_density: NDVI between 25th and 75th percentile
         high_density:   NDVI > 75th percentile
    4. Find centroid of each density cluster (use ee.Reducer.centroid()).
    5. Calculate plot radius based on land area:
         area = land.area_hectares
         if area <= 0.4:   radius = 7
         elif area <= 1.2: radius = 9
         else:             radius = 11
    6. Calculate linear walking path through zone centroids (nearest neighbour).
    7. Check if GEDI data exists within each zone:
         gedi = ee.ImageCollection('LARSE/GEDI/GEDI02_A_002_MONTHLY')
                .filterBounds(region).select(['rh98']).mean()
         gedi_coverage = check if any GEDI footprints exist in each zone
    8. Save zones to sampling_zones table. Create audit record with
       status='PROCESSING'. Returned zones are scan-required areas only;
       sparse canopy portions without a returned zone remain satellite-only.
    9. Return zones.
  Response:  {
               "audit_id": "uuid",
               "zones": [
                 {
                   "zone_id": "uuid",
                   "label": "A",
                   "centre_gps": { "lat": 18.546, "lng": 73.981 },
                   "radius_metres": 9,
                   "zone_type": "high_density",
                   "sequence_order": 1,
                   "gedi_available": true
                 },
                 ...
               ],
               "walking_path_metres": 145,
               "min_trees_required": 9
             }
  Note:      Example above is a 3-zone parcel, so min_trees_required = 9.
             gedi_available is determined per zone. When true, the backend
             resolves height from the nearest valid GEDI sample inside that
             zone and the app keeps height capture hidden. When false, the
             app must collect AR height for every tree in that zone.
  Error 409: { "error": "An audit already exists for this land in the current audit year.",
               "existing_audit_id": "uuid",
               "status": "CALCULATING" }

POST /api/v1/audit/submit-samples
  Purpose:   Receives all AR tree scan data. Stores it. Triggers background
             satellite fusion computation via Celery.
  Auth:      Required
  Request:   {
               "land_id": "uuid",
               "audit_id": "uuid",
               "trees": [
                 {
                   "zone_id": "uuid",
                   "species": "Teak",
                   "species_confidence": 0.91,
                   "species_source": "MODEL_AUTO",
                   "dbh_cm": 22.4,
                   "height_m": null,        // null = use GEDI
                   "gps": { "lat": 18.5461, "lng": 73.9812 },
                    "gps_accuracy_m": 4.2,  // horizontal accuracy in metres
                   "ar_tier_used": 1,
                   "confidence_score": 0.87,
                   "evidence_photo_base64": "[base64 of final JPEG bytes only, no data URI prefix, max 1280x960, EXIF stripped, JPEG quality 85]",
                   "evidence_photo_hash": "[sha256 hex string of the exact decoded bytes above]",
                   "scan_timestamp": "2026-11-10T10:24:31Z"
                 }
               ]
             }
  Process:
     1. Validate minimum tree count (must be >= 3 per generated zone;
       min_trees_required = zone_count x 3).
       Validate species according to species_source:
         MODEL_AUTO      requires species_confidence >= 0.80
         MODEL_CONFIRMED requires 0.60 <= species_confidence < 0.80
         MANUAL_SELECTED requires species to be in the approved list
                          even when model confidence is lower
       Reject any tree with gps_accuracy_m > 30.
       Reject any payload where evidence_photo_hash does not match the
       SHA-256 of the decoded, EXIF-stripped, final JPEG bytes provided.
    2. For each tree:
         a. Look up wood_density from species constants table.
         b. Store photo to Supabase Storage:
            path = f'{audit_id}/{tree_id}.jpg'
            supabase.storage.from_('evidence-photos').upload(path, photo_bytes)
         c. Insert into ar_tree_scans table.
    3. Update carbon_audits status to 'CALCULATING'.
    4. Dispatch Celery background task:
         fusion_task.delay(audit_id, land_id)
     5. Return immediately — do not wait for computation.
       If the same audit_id is submitted again while the audit is still in a
       non-terminal state, return the current processing response and do not
       create duplicate jobs.
  Response:  {
               "status": "PROCESSING",
               "audit_id": "uuid",
               "estimated_seconds": 60,
               "message": "Satellite verification in progress"
             }
  Error 400: { "error": "Minimum [min_trees_required] trees required for this audit." }

GET /api/v1/audit/result/{audit_id}
  Purpose:   Returns current status and result of audit computation.
             Mobile app polls this every 5 seconds after submission.
  Auth:      Required
  Response (non-terminal):
             { "status": "PROCESSING" } OR
             { "status": "CALCULATING" } OR
             { "status": "READY_TO_MINT" }
  Response (complete, no credits):
             {
               "status": "COMPLETE_NO_CREDITS",
               "total_biomass_tonnes": 14.8,
               "credits_issued": 0,
               "audit_year": 2026,
               "reason": "No biomass growth detected compared to the latest prior successful audit"
             }
  Response (complete):
             {
               "status": "MINTED",
               "total_biomass_tonnes": 14.8,
               "credits_issued": 2.4,
               "tx_hash": "0x1a2b3c...",
               "ipfs_certificate_url": "https://[pinata-gateway]/ipfs/[CID]",
               "audit_year": 2026
             }
  Response (failed):
             { "status": "FAILED",
               "error": "Insufficient satellite data for this region." }

GET /api/v1/audit/history/{land_id}
  Purpose:   Returns full audit history for a specific land parcel.
             Used for parcel-specific drill-down or operational support.

             GET /api/v1/credits/balance for the all-parcels history view.
  Auth:      Required
  Query:     page=1&limit=20
  Process:   Fetch carbon_audits rows for land_id, ordered by audit_year DESC.
  Response:  {
               "items": [
                 {
                   "audit_year": 2025,
                   "total_biomass_tonnes": 14.8,
                   "credits_issued": 2.4,
                   "tx_hash": "0x...",
                   "ipfs_certificate_url": "https://[pinata-gateway]/ipfs/[CID]",
                   "minted_at": "2025-11-15T14:23:00Z"
                 }
               ],
               "page": 1,
               "limit": 20,
               "total": 4,
               "has_more": false
             }
