================================================================================
SECTION 6: SATELLITE FUSION ENGINE

v4.0 correction: smileGradientTreeBoost is GEE_SMILE_GBT, not XGBOOST; focal_median
is focal median, not a Lee filter. Examples below are legacy farmer design and
not validated campus logic. Section 17 governs shared adapter/campus methodology;
no forced farmer model or sampling migration is part of institution delivery.
================================================================================

6.1 OVERVIEW
------------
The fusion engine is the scientific core of TerraTrust-AR. It takes the AR tree
scan measurements as ground truth and uses them to calibrate a multi-source
satellite model that predicts biomass for every 10m × 10m area of the farm.

It runs as a Celery background task, invoked after the farmer submits scans.
It typically takes 30-90 seconds to complete.

The engine uses 6 satellite data sources in combination. All are free and
available on Google Earth Engine. The XGBoost machine learning model learns
the relationship between the 12 satellite features and actual measured biomass
at the scan locations, then predicts biomass for the entire farm.

6.2 PHASED SAR USAGE
---------------------
Before June 2026:
  Primary SAR: Sentinel-1 C-band (VH + VV bands)
  NISAR: Available for development/testing only (pre-calibration data)
  Reason: NISAR calibrated data release expected June 2026

After June 2026:
  Primary SAR: NISAR L-band (HH + HV bands + ratio)
  Supplementary: Sentinel-1 C-band
  Reason: L-band penetrates full canopy without saturation, giving better
  biomass estimates in high-density tree areas.
  Estimated error improvement: ~35% → ~5-7%

6.3 WOOD DENSITY CONSTANTS (Chave Equation Parameter)
------------------------------------------------------
These values are used in Chave's allometric equation: AGB = 0.0673 *
(wood_density * DBH^2 * Height)^0.976. They are looked up by species name.

  SPECIES_WOOD_DENSITY = {
      "Teak":            0.60,   # Tectona grandis
      "Eucalyptus":      0.55,   # Eucalyptus spp.
      "Neem":            0.56,   # Azadirachta indica
      "Mango":           0.54,   # Mangifera indica
      "Bamboo":          0.70,   # Bambusa spp.
      "Pongamia":        0.67,   # Pongamia pinnata
      "Subabul":         0.56,   # Leucaena leucocephala
      "Casuarina":       0.69,   # Casuarina equisetifolia
      "Indian Rosewood": 0.75,   # Dalbergia sissoo
      "Drumstick":       0.39,   # Moringa oleifera
      "Amla":            0.74,   # Phyllanthus emblica
  }

Any species not in this dictionary should be rejected at the mobile app level
(TF Lite shows "not approved" message).
Canonical naming rule:
The display label "Indian Rosewood" refers specifically to Dalbergia sissoo.
Use the scientific identity as the canonical key and treat "Indian Rosewood",
"Shisham", and "North Indian Rosewood" as display aliases for that same
approved species only.
Mobile TFLite contract:
Input = RGB image, 224x224, normalized to [0,1]. Output = a fixed-order
probability vector of 12 labels: the 11 approved species above plus
NOT_APPROVED. The versioned mobile artifact in
src/assets/tflite/species_model.tflite must ship with the matching class
mapping for that model version. The backend never trains or runs this model at
runtime; it validates the submitted approved label together with
species_source and species_confidence.
The mobile model is trained offline from a curated labeled species dataset and
exported as a versioned TFLite artifact. The backend does not train this model
at runtime; it only validates the approved label set and the documented
species_source contract.

6.4 COMPLETE FUSION ENGINE CODE
---------------------------------

  # services/fusion_engine.py

  import ee
  import xgboost as xgb
  import numpy as np
  import asf_search as asf
  from typing import List, Dict

  SPECIES_WOOD_DENSITY = { ... }   # From Section 6.3

  def run_fusion(audit_id: str, land_id: str, tree_scans: List[Dict],
                 land_boundary_geojson: Dict, audit_year: int) -> Dict:

      # --- INITIALISE GEE ---
        ee.Initialize(project=GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT)

      start_date = f'{audit_year}-01-01'
      end_date   = f'{audit_year}-12-31'

      region = ee.Geometry.Polygon(land_boundary_geojson['coordinates'])

      # ====================================================================
      # LAYER 1: NISAR L-BAND (Primary SAR — After June 2026)
      # ====================================================================
      # Before June 2026: skip NISAR, use Sentinel-1 as primary
        nisar_available = check_nisar_availability(
          land_boundary_geojson, start_date, end_date
        )
        nisar_ready = os.getenv('NISAR_PRODUCTION_READY', 'false').lower() == 'true'

        if nisar_available and nisar_ready:
          nisar_hh, nisar_hv, nisar_ratio = fetch_nisar_data(
            land_boundary_geojson, start_date, end_date
          )
      else:
          nisar_hh = nisar_hv = nisar_ratio = None

      # ====================================================================
      # LAYER 2: SENTINEL-1 C-BAND SAR (Always used, supplementary after 2026)
      # ====================================================================
      s1 = ee.ImageCollection('COPERNICUS/S1_GRD') \
            .filterBounds(region) \
            .filterDate(start_date, end_date) \
            .filter(ee.Filter.eq('instrumentMode', 'IW')) \
            .filter(ee.Filter.listContains('transmitterReceiverPolarisation', 'VH')) \
            .select(['VH', 'VV']) \
            .median()

      # CRITICAL: Apply speckle filter to reduce radar noise
      # Lee speckle filter — 3x3 kernel
      s1_filtered = s1.focal_median(radius=1, kernelType='square', units='pixels')
      s1_vh = s1_filtered.select('VH')
      s1_vv = s1_filtered.select('VV')
      s1_ratio = s1_vh.divide(s1_vv).rename('VH_VV_RATIO')

      # ====================================================================
      # LAYER 3: SENTINEL-2 OPTICAL (Annual cloud-free composite)
      # ====================================================================
      s2 = ee.ImageCollection('COPERNICUS/S2_SR_HARMONIZED') \
            .filterBounds(region) \
            .filterDate(start_date, end_date) \
            .filter(ee.Filter.lt('CLOUDY_PIXEL_PERCENTAGE', 20)) \
            .median()

      # NDVI: Normalized Difference Vegetation Index
      # Formula: (NIR - Red) / (NIR + Red) — Bands B8, B4
      ndvi = s2.normalizedDifference(['B8', 'B4']).rename('NDVI')

      # EVI: Enhanced Vegetation Index (does not saturate in high biomass)
      # Formula: 2.5 * (NIR - Red) / (NIR + 6*Red - 7.5*Blue + 1)
      evi = s2.expression(
          '2.5 * ((NIR - RED) / (NIR + 6 * RED - 7.5 * BLUE + 1))',
          {'NIR': s2.select('B8'), 'RED': s2.select('B4'), 'BLUE': s2.select('B2')}
      ).rename('EVI')

      # Red-Edge Band B7 — proven to improve accuracy in plantation forests
      red_edge = s2.select('B7').rename('RED_EDGE')

      # ====================================================================
      # LAYER 4: GEDI CANOPY HEIGHT (Where footprints exist)
      # ====================================================================
      # rh98 = height at which 98% of returned laser energy is below
      # = effective canopy top height = tree height from space
      gedi = ee.ImageCollection('LARSE/GEDI/GEDI02_A_002_MONTHLY') \
              .filterBounds(region) \
              .select(['rh98']) \
              .mean() \
              .rename('GEDI_HEIGHT')

      gedi_has_land_coverage = image_has_valid_pixels(gedi, region)

      # ====================================================================
      # LAYER 5: SRTM TOPOGRAPHY (Terrain correction)
      # ====================================================================
      # SRTM: Shuttle Radar Topography Mission — global 30m elevation data
      # Used to correct radar signal distortion caused by terrain slope
      srtm = ee.Image('USGS/SRTMGL1_003')
      elevation = srtm.select('elevation').rename('ELEVATION')
      slope = ee.Terrain.slope(srtm).rename('SLOPE')
      # Slope: steeper slope = radar signal distorted, may over/under estimate

      # ====================================================================
      # STACK ALL FEATURES INTO ONE IMAGE
      # ====================================================================
        if nisar_available and nisar_hh is not None and gedi_has_land_coverage:
          feature_stack = ee.Image.cat([
              nisar_hh,         # Feature 1: NISAR HH backscatter
              nisar_hv,         # Feature 2: NISAR HV backscatter
              nisar_ratio,      # Feature 3: NISAR HH/HV ratio
              s1_vh,            # Feature 4: Sentinel-1 VH
              s1_vv,            # Feature 5: Sentinel-1 VV
              s1_ratio,         # Feature 6: S1 VH/VV ratio
              ndvi,             # Feature 7: Sentinel-2 NDVI
              evi,              # Feature 8: Sentinel-2 EVI
              red_edge,         # Feature 9: Sentinel-2 Red-Edge
              gedi,             # Feature 10: GEDI canopy height
              elevation,        # Feature 11: SRTM elevation
              slope             # Feature 12: SRTM slope
          ])
            elif nisar_available and nisar_hh is not None and not gedi_has_land_coverage:
              # GEDI absent across the parcel: use 11 features without GEDI
              feature_stack = ee.Image.cat([
                nisar_hh, nisar_hv, nisar_ratio,
                s1_vh, s1_vv, s1_ratio,
                ndvi, evi, red_edge,
                elevation, slope
              ])
            elif gedi_has_land_coverage:
              # Pre-NISAR or NISAR disabled: 9 features
          feature_stack = ee.Image.cat([
              s1_vh, s1_vv, s1_ratio,
              ndvi, evi, red_edge,
              gedi, elevation, slope
          ])
            else:
              # GEDI absent across the parcel: use 8 features and rely on
              # AR-measured heights only for tree-level Chave calculations.
              feature_stack = ee.Image.cat([
                s1_vh, s1_vv, s1_ratio,
                ndvi, evi, red_edge,
                elevation, slope
              ])

      # ====================================================================
      # GROUND TRUTH: CHAVE'S ALLOMETRIC EQUATION
      # ====================================================================
      training_points = []
      for scan in tree_scans:
          # Get tree height — prefer GEDI, fall back to AR measurement
          if scan.get('gedi_height_m') and scan['gedi_height_m'] > 0:
              height = scan['gedi_height_m']
              height_source = 'GEDI'
          else:
              height = scan['height_m']
              height_source = 'AR_FALLBACK'

          if scan['species'] not in SPECIES_WOOD_DENSITY:
              raise ValueError(f"Unsupported species: {scan['species']}")
          wood_density = SPECIES_WOOD_DENSITY[scan['species']]
          dbh = scan['dbh_cm']

          # Chave's Allometric Equation (Chave et al., 2014)
          # AGB = 0.0673 * (rho * D^2 * H)^0.976
          # rho = wood density (g/cm3), D = DBH (cm), H = height (m)
          # Output: AGB in kg
          agb_kg = 0.0673 * (wood_density * (dbh ** 2) * height) ** 0.976

          # Convert to tonnes per hectare using one 10m x 10m satellite pixel
          # as the mapping unit (0.01 ha).
          # Simplifying assumption: each scanned tree represents one pixel,
          # not its literal canopy/crown area.
          agb_t_per_ha = (agb_kg / 1000) / 0.01

          training_points.append({
              'lat': scan['gps']['lat'],
              'lng': scan['gps']['lng'],
              'agb_t_per_ha': agb_t_per_ha,
              'height_source': height_source
          })

          # Update scan record with calculated AGB
          update_scan_agb(scan['id'], agb_kg, height_source)

      # ====================================================================
      # TRAIN XGBOOST ON AR GROUND TRUTH + SATELLITE FEATURES
      # ====================================================================
        # Build a lightweight label collection, then attach all satellite bands
        # in one sampleRegions() call to avoid per-tree getInfo() requests.
        points_fc = ee.FeatureCollection([
          ee.Feature(
            ee.Geometry.Point([pt['lng'], pt['lat']]),
            {
              'agb_t_per_ha': pt['agb_t_per_ha'],
              'height_source': pt['height_source'],
            }
          )
          for pt in training_points
        ])

        training_fc = feature_stack.sampleRegions(
          collection=points_fc,
          properties=['agb_t_per_ha', 'height_source'],
          scale=10,
          geometries=False
        )

      # XGBoost Gradient Boosted Trees (replaces single k-factor approach)
      # Uses GEE's built-in smileGradientTreeBoost classifier
      xgb_model = ee.Classifier.smileGradientTreeBoost(
          numberOfTrees=100,      # 100 decision trees
          shrinkage=0.05,         # Learning rate
          samplingRate=0.7,       # Use 70% of data per tree (prevents overfitting)
          maxNodes=None           # Let tree depth be determined automatically
      ).setOutputMode('REGRESSION').train(
          features=training_fc,
          classProperty='agb_t_per_ha',
          inputProperties=feature_stack.bandNames()
      )

      # ====================================================================
      # PREDICT BIOMASS FOR EVERY PIXEL IN THE LAND BOUNDARY
      # ====================================================================
      # Each pixel = 10m x 10m = 100 m2
      # Predicted value = AGB in tonnes per hectare for that pixel
      biomass_map = feature_stack.classify(xgb_model)

      # Sum all pixel values * pixel area to get total farm biomass
      pixel_area_ha = 0.01   # 100 m2 = 0.01 hectares

      total_biomass_result = biomass_map.multiply(pixel_area_ha).reduceRegion(
          reducer=ee.Reducer.sum(),
          geometry=region,
          scale=10,
          maxPixels=1e9
      ).getInfo()

      total_biomass_tonnes = total_biomass_result.get('classification', 0)

      return {
          'total_biomass_tonnes': total_biomass_tonnes,
          'training_points_count': len(training_points),
          'features_used': 12 if nisar_available else 9,
          'nisar_used': nisar_available
      }


  def fetch_nisar_data(geojson, start_date, end_date):
      """
      Fetch NISAR L-band data from Alaska Satellite Facility.
      Available from October 2025.
      Treat as development/validation input until calibrated production
      readiness is confirmed, targeted from June 2026 onward.
      """
      import asf_search as asf
      from shapely.geometry import shape

      polygon = shape(geojson)
      wkt = polygon.wkt

      results = asf.search(
          platform=asf.PLATFORM.NISAR,
          intersectsWith=wkt,
          processingLevel='GCOV',   # Geocoded Covariance — best for biomass
          start=start_date,
          end=end_date
      )

      if not results:
          return None, None, None

      # Download and process NISAR data
      # Then ingest as GEE image for consistent pixel-level operations
      # This is a simplified representation — actual implementation requires
      # downloading HDF5 files, extracting HH/HV bands, and uploading to GEE
      # as an Earth Engine asset.

      # Once ingested as GEE asset:
      nisar_img = ee.Image(f'users/terratrust/nisar_{start_date[:4]}')
      hh = nisar_img.select('HH').rename('NISAR_HH')
      hv = nisar_img.select('HV').rename('NISAR_HV')
      ratio = hh.divide(hv).rename('NISAR_HH_HV_RATIO')

      return hh, hv, ratio

6.5 CARBON CREDIT CALCULATION
-------------------------------
Run after fusion engine returns total_biomass_tonnes:

  def calculate_credits(total_biomass_tonnes: float,
                         land_id: str,
                         audit_year: int) -> Dict:

        # Query the latest prior successful audit, not simply audit_year - 1.
        # Ignore FAILED / PROCESSING records.
        prev_audit = get_latest_successful_audit_before(land_id, audit_year)

        if not prev_audit:
          return {
            'prev_biomass': 0,
            'current_biomass': total_biomass_tonnes,
            'delta_biomass': 0,
            'carbon_tonnes': 0,
            'co2_equivalent': 0,
            'credits_issued': 0,
            'reason': 'Baseline year established; future growth earns credits'
          }

        prev_biomass = prev_audit['total_biomass_tonnes']

      delta_biomass = total_biomass_tonnes - prev_biomass

      if delta_biomass <= 0:
          # No growth — no credits earned
          return {
              'credits_issued': 0,
              'reason': 'No biomass growth detected compared to previous year'
          }

      # Carbon fraction of dry biomass = 0.47 (IPCC standard value)
      carbon_tonnes = delta_biomass * 0.47

      # CO2 equivalent: carbon * (44/12) = carbon * 3.667
      co2_equivalent = carbon_tonnes * 3.667

      # credits_issued is the human-readable CTT amount.
      # Minting converts it to integer deci-CTT units on-chain.
      credits_issued = co2_equivalent

      return {
          'prev_biomass': prev_biomass,
          'current_biomass': total_biomass_tonnes,
          'delta_biomass': delta_biomass,
          'carbon_tonnes': carbon_tonnes,
          'co2_equivalent': co2_equivalent,
          'credits_issued': credits_issued
      }

================================================================================
SECTION 7: BLOCKCHAIN ARCHITECTURE

Farmer-only. Institution stock/balance/advisor never calls these minting services.
Institution report/evidence is separate and private; on-chain anchoring is LATER.
================================================================================

7.1 WHY BLOCKCHAIN
------------------
Blockchain is used exclusively as a tamper-proof permanent record. Once a
carbon credit is minted on Polygon, the record cannot be edited, deleted, or
disputed. Carbon market buyers can verify every credit's origin, measurement
data, and land ownership proof by reading the on-chain certificate.

7.2 TOKEN STANDARD: ERC-1155
------------------------------
ERC-1155 (Multi-Token Standard) is used because the system needs two types
of tokens per audit:

Type 1 — Carbon Credit Token (Fungible, ID=1)
  Fungible means all credits are interchangeable. 1 CTT from Farm A is worth
  exactly the same as 1 CTT from Farm B. Like currency notes.
  These are the actual tradable carbon credits.

Type 2 — Audit Certificate NFT (Non-Fungible, unique ID per audit)
  Each audit certificate is unique — it represents a specific farm, a specific
  year, with specific measurement data linked via IPFS.
  These prove how and where each batch of credits was generated.

Both are minted in one transaction, saving on blockchain fees.

7.3 WHAT GETS STORED ON IPFS (via Pinata)
-------------------------------------------
Before minting, the backend creates a JSON metadata package containing all
evidence for the audit. This is uploaded to IPFS via Pinata's
pinJSONToIPFS endpoint. The returned CID (permanent content address) is stored
on blockchain as ipfs://CID, while the API returns a Pinata gateway URL for
certificate viewing inside the app.

IPFS metadata package structure:
  {
    "name": "TerraTrust Audit — Farm [land_id], Year [year]",
    "land_id": "uuid",
    "survey_number": "47",
    "district": "Pune",
    "land_boundary_hash": "sha256 of GeoJSON string",
    "audit_year": 2026,
    "measurement_date": "2026-11-10",
    "total_biomass_tonnes": 14.8,
    "prev_biomass_tonnes": 12.4,
    "delta_biomass": 2.4,
    "carbon_tonnes": 1.128,
    "co2_equivalent": 4.1,
    "credits_issued": 2.4,
    "satellite_data": {
      "sentinel1_vh_mean_db": -12.4,
      "sentinel2_ndvi_mean": 0.68,
      "gedi_height_mean": 14.2,
      "srtm_elevation_mean": 545.0,
      "nisar_used": true,
      "features_count": 12,
      "processing_method": "NISAR_S1_S2_GEDI_SRTM_XGBoost_v3.1"
    },
    "tree_samples": [
      {
        "species": "Teak",
        "dbh_cm": 22.4,
        "height_source": "GEDI",
        "height_m": 15.3,
        "agb_kg": 245.6,
        "gps": { "lat": 18.546, "lng": 73.981 },
        "scan_timestamp": "2026-11-10T10:24:31Z",
        "measurement_tier": 1,
        "evidence_photo_hash": "sha256..."
      }
    ],
    "boundary_verification_method": "WMS_AUTO"
  }

7.4 THE COMPLETE SMART CONTRACT (TerraToken.sol)
-------------------------------------------------

UUID-to-uint256 conversion rule:
  audit_token_id = uuid.UUID(audit_id).int
  Use this exact conversion in the backend before calling mintAudit().

  // SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
  pragma solidity 0.8.29;

  import "@openzeppelin/contracts/token/ERC1155/ERC1155.sol";
  import "@openzeppelin/contracts/access/Ownable.sol";

  contract TerraTrustToken is ERC1155, Ownable {

      // ID = 1 is used for all fungible carbon credit tokens
      uint256 public constant CARBON_CREDIT = 1;

      // Stores IPFS evidence link per audit certificate
      // auditId => "ipfs://CID..."
      mapping(uint256 => string) public auditEvidence;

      // Prevents minting the same land+year combination twice
      // keccak256(landId + auditYear) => bool
      mapping(bytes32 => bool) public auditMinted;

      // Tracks how many credits each address has permanently retired
      mapping(address => uint256) public retiredCredits;

      // Emitted every time credits are permanently retired
      // Creates permanent public record for carbon market compliance
      event CreditRetired(
          address indexed retiredBy,
          uint256 amount,
          string retirementReason,
          uint256 timestamp
      );

      constructor() ERC1155("") Ownable(msg.sender) {}

      // ================================================================
      // MINT FUNCTION — called only by backend Oracle wallet
      // ================================================================
      function mintAudit(
          address farmer,        // Farmer's wallet address
          uint256 auditId,       // uuid.UUID(audit_id).int
          uint256 creditAmount,  // Integer deci-CTT units e.g. 124 for 12.4 CTT
                     // NO 10^18 scaling; backend sends display_ctt * 10
          string memory ipfsHash, // "ipfs://CID..." — points to evidence
          string memory landId,  // Internal land UUID as string
          uint256 auditYear      // e.g. 2026
      ) public onlyOwner {

          // PREVENT DOUBLE MINTING
          // Same land cannot be minted twice in the same year
          bytes32 auditKey = keccak256(abi.encodePacked(landId, auditYear));
          require(!auditMinted[auditKey],
              "Credits already minted for this land this year");
          auditMinted[auditKey] = true;

          // Mint fungible carbon credit tokens to farmer
          // creditAmount is raw deci-CTT units (e.g. 124 = 12.4 CTT)
          _mint(farmer, CARBON_CREDIT, creditAmount, "");

          // Mint unique audit certificate NFT to farmer
          _mint(farmer, auditId, 1, "");

          // Store IPFS link permanently on-chain
          auditEvidence[auditId] = ipfsHash;
      }

      // ================================================================
      // RETIRE FUNCTION — called by carbon credit buyer
      // ================================================================
      // When a company buys credits to offset emissions, they must RETIRE
      // (permanently burn) those credits to prove usage. Without this,
      // the same credit could be sold twice. All major carbon markets
      // (Verra, Gold Standard) require on-chain retirement records.
      function retireCredits(
          uint256 amount,
          string memory reason   // "Offset for Q1 2026 manufacturing emissions"
      ) public {

          require(
              balanceOf(msg.sender, CARBON_CREDIT) >= amount,
              "Insufficient credits to retire"
          );

          // Permanently burn — cannot be undone
          _burn(msg.sender, CARBON_CREDIT, amount);

          // Record total retired by this address
          retiredCredits[msg.sender] += amount;

          // Emit permanent public retirement record
          emit CreditRetired(msg.sender, amount, reason, block.timestamp);
      }
  }

7.6 DEPLOYMENT SCRIPT
----------------------

  // contracts/scripts/deploy.js
  const hre = require("hardhat");
  const fs = require("fs");
  const path = require("path");

  async function main() {
    const [deployer] = await hre.ethers.getSigners();
    console.log("Deploying TerraTrustToken with account:", deployer.address);

    const balance = await hre.ethers.provider.getBalance(deployer.address);
    console.log("Account balance:", balance.toString());

    const Token = await hre.ethers.getContractFactory("TerraTrustToken");
    const token = await Token.deploy();
    await token.waitForDeployment();

    const contractAddress = await token.getAddress();

    console.log("TerraTrustToken deployed to:", contractAddress);

    const artifact = await hre.artifacts.readArtifact("TerraTrustToken");
    const outputPaths = [
      path.join(__dirname, "..", "artifacts", "TerraTrustToken_ABI.json"),
      path.join(__dirname, "..", "artifacts", "TerraToken_ABI.json")
    ];
    outputPaths.forEach((outputPath) => {
      fs.mkdirSync(path.dirname(outputPath), { recursive: true });
      fs.writeFileSync(outputPath, JSON.stringify(artifact.abi, null, 2));
      console.log("ABI exported to:", outputPath);
    });
  }

  main().catch((error) => {
    console.error(error);
    process.exitCode = 1;
  });

  // Run with:
  // npx hardhat run scripts/deploy.js --network polygon_amoy
  // After success, verify on PolygonScan:
  // npx hardhat verify --network polygon_amoy [CONTRACT_ADDRESS]

7.7 PYTHON MINTING SERVICE
---------------------------

  # services/minting_service.py
  import json, requests
  from web3 import Web3
  from eth_account import Account
  import os

  w3 = Web3(Web3.HTTPProvider(os.getenv('ALCHEMY_POLYGON_AMOY_URL')))
  admin_account = Account.from_key(os.getenv('ADMIN_WALLET_PRIVATE_KEY'))

  with open('./contracts/artifacts/TerraToken_ABI.json') as f:
      CONTRACT_ABI = json.load(f)

  contract = w3.eth.contract(
      address=w3.to_checksum_address(os.getenv('CONTRACT_ADDRESS')),
      abi=CONTRACT_ABI
  )

  def upload_to_ipfs(metadata: dict) -> str:
      """Upload audit evidence JSON to Pinata. Returns IPFS URI."""
      response = requests.post(
        'https://api.pinata.cloud/pinning/pinJSONToIPFS',
          headers={
          'Authorization': f'Bearer {os.getenv("PINATA_JWT")}',
              'Content-Type': 'application/json'
          },
        json={
          'pinataContent': metadata,
          'pinataMetadata': {
            'name': f'audit-{metadata["audit_year"]}-{metadata["land_id"]}'
          }
        },
          timeout=30
      )
      response.raise_for_status()
      cid = response.json()['IpfsHash']
      return f'ipfs://{cid}'

  def mint_carbon_credits(farmer_address: str, audit_id_int: int,
                           credit_amount: int, metadata: dict,
                           land_id: str, audit_year: int) -> Dict:
      """
      Complete minting flow:
      1. Upload evidence to IPFS
      2. Build blockchain transaction
      3. Sign and send transaction
      4. Wait for confirmation
      5. Return tx_hash
      """
      # Step 1: Upload evidence to IPFS
      ipfs_url = upload_to_ipfs(metadata)

      # Step 2: Build transaction
      nonce = w3.eth.get_transaction_count(admin_account.address)
      gas_price = w3.eth.gas_price

      transaction = contract.functions.mintAudit(
          w3.to_checksum_address(farmer_address),
          audit_id_int,
          credit_amount,    # Actual number, no multiplication
          ipfs_url,
          land_id,
          audit_year
      ).build_transaction({
          'from':     admin_account.address,
          'nonce':    nonce,
          'gas':      300000,
          'gasPrice': gas_price,
          'chainId':  80002   # Amoy testnet only in current TerraTrust scope
      })

      # Step 3: Sign and send
      signed_txn = admin_account.sign_transaction(transaction)
      tx_hash = w3.eth.send_raw_transaction(signed_txn.raw_transaction)

      # Step 4: Wait for confirmation (up to 60 seconds)
      receipt = w3.eth.wait_for_transaction_receipt(tx_hash, timeout=60)

      if receipt['status'] == 0:
          raise Exception(f"Transaction failed: {tx_hash.hex()}")

      return {
          'tx_hash': tx_hash.hex(),
          'ipfs_url': ipfs_url,
          'block_number': receipt['blockNumber'],
          'gas_used': receipt['gasUsed']
      }

================================================================================
SECTION 8: CELERY BACKGROUND TASK SYSTEM
================================================================================

8.1 WHY CELERY
--------------
Satellite fusion takes 30-90 seconds. Blockchain transactions can take 15-30
seconds. These cannot run inside a synchronous API request (client would time
out). Celery runs them as background jobs. The mobile app polls for results.

8.2 SETUP
---------

  # tasks/celery_app.py
  from celery import Celery
  import os

  celery_app = Celery(
      'terratrust',
      broker=os.getenv('REDIS_URL', 'redis://localhost:6379/0'),
      backend=os.getenv('REDIS_URL', 'redis://localhost:6379/0'),
      include=['tasks.fusion_task', 'tasks.minting_task']
  )

  celery_app.conf.update(
      task_serializer='json',
      result_serializer='json',
      accept_content=['json'],
      task_track_started=True,
      task_time_limit=300,       # Kill task if it runs more than 5 minutes
      worker_prefetch_multiplier=1
  )

  Free-tier worker note:
    Start with Celery concurrency=1 on Render free/low-memory instances.
    Increase above 1 only after measuring memory headroom for GEE + OCR workloads.
    task_time_limit=300 is an MVP safeguard, not a hard scientific upper bound.
    If NISAR-enabled or quota-delayed runs approach that limit in practice,
    increase it deliberately and document the new bound before production.

Free Redis hosting options:
  - Upstash Redis free tier: serverless Redis over TLS via REDIS_URL
  - Local Redis: redis://localhost:6379/0 for development only

8.3 FUSION TASK
---------------

  # tasks/fusion_task.py
  from tasks.celery_app import celery_app
  from services.fusion_engine import run_fusion, calculate_credits
  from services.minting_service import mint_carbon_credits
  from app.database import update_audit_status, get_audit_data

  @celery_app.task(bind=True, max_retries=2)
  def run_audit_fusion(self, audit_id: str):
      try:
          # Update status: CALCULATING
          update_audit_status(audit_id, 'CALCULATING')

          # Fetch all data needed for fusion
          audit_data = get_audit_data(audit_id)

          # Run satellite fusion
          fusion_result = run_fusion(
              audit_id=audit_id,
              land_id=audit_data['land_id'],
              tree_scans=audit_data['tree_scans'],
              land_boundary_geojson=audit_data['boundary'],
              audit_year=audit_data['audit_year']
          )

          # Calculate carbon credits from biomass result
          credit_result = calculate_credits(
              total_biomass_tonnes=fusion_result['total_biomass_tonnes'],
              land_id=audit_data['land_id'],
              audit_year=audit_data['audit_year']
          )

          # Save results to database
          save_audit_results(audit_id, fusion_result, credit_result)

          # If credits > 0, trigger minting
          if credit_result['credits_issued'] > 0:
              update_audit_status(audit_id, 'READY_TO_MINT')
              run_minting_task.delay(audit_id)
          else:
              update_audit_status(audit_id, 'COMPLETE_NO_CREDITS')

      except Exception as exc:
          update_audit_status(audit_id, 'FAILED')
          raise self.retry(exc=exc, countdown=30)

================================================================================
SECTION 9: OCR SERVICE — LAND DOCUMENT PROCESSING
================================================================================

9.1 SETUP REQUIREMENTS
-----------------------

No system-level OCR installation is required. The backend uses the managed
Google Cloud Vision API and authenticates with the shared backend Google
service account exposed through GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS.

Install on server:
  pip install google-cloud-vision opencv-python-headless Pillow

Deployment note:
  There is no first-run OCR model download. The only setup requirement is:
    - Cloud Vision API enabled in GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT
    - GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS pointing to the shared backend JSON file

On Render.com free tier:
  No GPU or OCR model bootstrap is needed. The backend sends OCR requests to
  Cloud Vision over HTTPS.

9.2 PREPROCESSING PIPELINE
----------------------------

Note on preprocessing vs previous version:
  OpenCV now handles image cleanup before calling Cloud Vision. Orientation is
  normalised from image metadata when available, and the backend can retry OCR
  with the original bytes if the processed image performs worse.

  # services/ocr_service.py
  import cv2
  import numpy as np
  import re
  from typing import Dict
  from google.cloud import vision

  vision_client = vision.ImageAnnotatorClient()

  def preprocess_document_image(image_bytes: bytes) -> bytes:
      """
      Prepare 7/12 document image for Cloud Vision.
      OpenCV is used for orientation normalisation, contrast cleanup,
      and denoising before OCR.
      """
      # Convert bytes to numpy array
      nparr = np.frombuffer(image_bytes, np.uint8)
      img = cv2.imdecode(nparr, cv2.IMREAD_COLOR)

      # Step 1: Convert to greyscale
      grey = cv2.cvtColor(img, cv2.COLOR_BGR2GRAY)

      # Step 2: Adaptive thresholding (handles uneven lighting and shadows)
      # Government documents are often photographed with uneven phone lighting
      thresh = cv2.adaptiveThreshold(grey, 255,
                                      cv2.ADAPTIVE_THRESH_GAUSSIAN_C,
                                      cv2.THRESH_BINARY, 11, 2)

      # Step 3: Denoise — remove camera noise from low-light photos
      denoised = cv2.fastNlMeansDenoising(thresh, h=10)

      # Step 4: Encode to PNG bytes for Vision API
      success, encoded = cv2.imencode('.png', denoised)
      if not success:
          raise ValueError("Could not prepare document image for OCR.")

      return encoded.tobytes()

  def extract_fields_from_document(image_bytes: bytes) -> Dict:
      """
      Run Cloud Vision OCR and extract structured fields from 7/12 document.
      Returns extracted fields dict or raises ValueError if extraction fails.
      """
      processed = preprocess_document_image(image_bytes)

      def detect_text(content: bytes) -> str:
          response = vision_client.document_text_detection(
              image=vision.Image(content=content)
          )
          if response.error.message:
              raise ValueError(f"Cloud Vision OCR failed: {response.error.message}")
          return response.full_text_annotation.text or ""

      # Try the cleaned image first, then fall back to the original image.
      full_text = detect_text(processed).strip()
      if len(full_text) < 10:
          full_text = detect_text(image_bytes).strip()

      # Extract fields using regex (handles both English and Marathi labels)
      survey_no = re.search(
          r'(?:Survey|Gat|Gut|सर्व्हे|गट)\s*[Nn]o\.?\s*[:\-]?\s*(\d+(?:/\d+)?)',
          full_text, re.IGNORECASE
      )
      owner = re.search(
          r'(?:Name of Owner|Owner|मालकाचे नाव|धारकाचे नाव)\s*[:\-]?\s*'
          r'([A-Z][a-zA-Z\s]+|[\u0900-\u097F\s]+)',
          full_text, re.IGNORECASE
      )
      village = re.search(
          r'(?:Village|गाव)\s*[:\-]?\s*([A-Z][a-zA-Z\s]+|[\u0900-\u097F\s]+)',
          full_text, re.IGNORECASE
      )
      taluka = re.search(
          r'(?:Taluka|तालुका)\s*[:\-]?\s*([A-Z][a-zA-Z\s]+|[\u0900-\u097F\s]+)',
          full_text, re.IGNORECASE
      )
      district = re.search(
          r'(?:District|जिल्हा)\s*[:\-]?\s*([A-Z][a-zA-Z\s]+|[\u0900-\u097F\s]+)',
          full_text, re.IGNORECASE
      )

      # Check minimum required fields were extracted
      if not survey_no or not owner:
          raise ValueError("Could not extract survey number or owner name. "
                           "Image quality may be too low.")

      # Calculate extraction confidence based on how many fields were found
      fields_found = sum([bool(x) for x in [survey_no, owner, village,
                                              taluka, district]])
      confidence = fields_found / 5

      return {
          'survey_number': survey_no.group(1).strip() if survey_no else None,
          'owner_name':    owner.group(1).strip() if owner else None,
          'village':       village.group(1).strip() if village else None,
          'taluka':        taluka.group(1).strip() if taluka else None,
          'district':      district.group(1).strip() if district else None,
          'state':         'Maharashtra',
          'extraction_confidence': confidence
      }

  # Terminal 1: Start FastAPI

  # Optional single-command layout matching Render deployment
  # backend/Procfile.render
  #   web: uvicorn main:app --host 0.0.0.0 --port ${PORT:-8000}
  #   worker: celery -A tasks.celery_app worker --loglevel=info --concurrency=1
  # Run with: honcho -f Procfile.render start

11.2 DOCKER (OPTIONAL FOR LOCAL)
----------------------------------

  # docker-compose.yml
  version: '3.8'
  services:

    backend:
      build: ./backend
      command: honcho -f Procfile.render start
      ports: ["8000:8000"]
      env_file: .env
      depends_on: [redis]
      volumes:
        - ./backend:/app
        - ./backend-google-service-account.json:/app/backend-google-service-account.json

    redis:
      image: redis:7-alpine
      ports: ["6379:6379"]

  # Run with:
  # docker-compose up --build

11.3 FREE CLOUD DEPLOYMENT OPTIONS
------------------------------------

Render.com + Upstash (Recommended for MVP hosting):
  - One Render web service runs both FastAPI and the Celery worker
  - Upstash Redis provides the Celery broker/result backend over TLS
  - Both connect to Supabase (already cloud-hosted)
  Steps:
   1. Push code to GitHub
   2. Create Render account at render.com
   3. New Web Service → Connect GitHub repo → Set build command:
     pip install -r requirements.txt
   4. Add backend/Procfile.render:
      web: uvicorn main:app --host 0.0.0.0 --port ${PORT:-8000}
      worker: celery -A tasks.celery_app worker --loglevel=info --concurrency=1
   5. Set start command: honcho -f Procfile.render start
   6. Add all environment variables from Section 1.11.
     Upload backend-google-service-account.json as a Render Secret File.
     Set GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS=/etc/secrets/backend-google-service-account.json
     and GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT=[your-google-cloud-project-id].
   7. Create an Upstash Redis database. Copy the rediss:// URL into REDIS_URL.
     Do NOT use Upstash REST credentials for Celery.

11.4 CORS CONFIGURATION
------------------------
React Native mobile clients do not require browser CORS. Add this middleware
only if a web dashboard is introduced later:

  from fastapi.middleware.cors import CORSMiddleware

  app.add_middleware(
      CORSMiddleware,
      allow_origins=os.getenv('WEB_CORS_ORIGINS', '').split(','),
      allow_credentials=True,
      allow_methods=["*"],
      allow_headers=["*"],
  )

================================================================================
SECTION 12: SECURITY
================================================================================

Private Key Storage:
  ADMIN_WALLET_PRIVATE_KEY must be stored only in environment variables.
  Never in code files. Never in git. Use Render.com environment variables
  section or a secrets manager in production.

Backend Data Access Policy:
  Mobile app never talks to Supabase directly and never receives
  SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY. All database and storage access runs through FastAPI.
  Authorization is enforced in backend code after resolving users.firebase_uid
  from a verified Firebase ID token. Supabase RLS can remain enabled as a
  defense-in-depth control, but it is not the primary authorization boundary
  in this architecture.

Firebase ID Token Validation:
  All protected endpoints validate Firebase ID tokens via the Authorization
  header. FastAPI dependency checks the token on every protected request:
    from firebase_admin import auth as firebase_auth

    async def get_current_user(token: str = Depends(oauth2_scheme)):
        decoded = firebase_auth.verify_id_token(token)
        firebase_uid = decoded['uid']
        phone_number = decoded.get('phone_number')
        user = get_user_by_firebase_uid(firebase_uid, phone_number)
        if not user:
            raise HTTPException(401, "Authenticated user not provisioned")
        return user

Google Credentials:
  backend-google-service-account.json never goes in git.
  In production, upload it as a Render Secret File and set
  GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS=/etc/secrets/backend-google-service-account.json.
  Reuse this one file for Firebase Admin, Cloud Vision, and Earth Engine.

Smart Contract:
  onlyOwner modifier ensures only the admin wallet can call mint().
  Double-mint mapping prevents duplicate credits per land per year.
  No user-facing function can mint tokens — only the backend Oracle.

13.1 UNIT TESTS

  # Run all tests
  pytest backend/tests/ -v

  # Test files to create:
  tests/
  ├── test_ocr_service.py         # Test field extraction with sample 7/12 images
  ├── test_fusion_engine.py       # Test Chave equation, credit calculation
  ├── test_land_boundary.py       # Test LGD API call, gis_code construction
  ├── test_minting_service.py     # Test IPFS upload, web3 transaction building
  └── test_blockchain_contract.py # Hardhat tests for Solidity contract

  Key test cases:
  - Chave equation: known inputs → known AGB output (verify against published data)
  - Credit calculation: delta_biomass = 0 → credits = 0 (no credits for no growth)
  - Credit calculation: negative delta → credits = 0 (no credits for decline)
  - OCR service: test with clear image → all fields extracted
  - OCR service: test with blurry image → appropriate error returned
  - Contract: mintAudit same land+year twice → second call reverts

SECTION 14: IMPLEMENTATION SEQUENCE

Historical farmer sequence retained for reference. Do not restart completed
farmer work; use additive institution gates in Section 22 and SRS 36.
================================================================================

Follow this order to build the backend. This backend schedule aligns to the
16-week implementation sequence in the SRS.

WEEKS 1-2 — FOUNDATION
  1. Set up all accounts from Section 1.
  2. Create Supabase project, enable PostGIS, and run all CREATE TABLE statements.
  3. Set up the FastAPI project structure from Section 3.
  4. Implement /health, Firebase token validation, /auth/me, /auth/kyc,
    and /auth/register-wallet.
  5. Test OTP bootstrap and KYC persistence with Firebase test numbers.

WEEKS 3-4 — LAND REGISTRATION
  1. Enable and validate Cloud Vision OCR with sample 7/12 images.
  2. Implement OCR extraction, LGD API integration, and BhuNaksha WMS lookup.
  3. Implement /land/verify-document, /land/fetch-boundary, /land/register,
    and paginated /land/list.
  4. Test the full OCR → boundary → registration flow, including manual fallback.

WEEKS 5-7 — AUDIT BACKEND INTERFACES
  1. Implement /audit/zones with NDVI-based zone generation and GEDI availability.
  2. Implement /audit/submit-samples with server-side geofence validation,
    photo hash validation contract, and Celery dispatch.
  3. Set up Celery + Redis, background job retries, and COMPLETE_NO_CREDITS flow.

WEEKS 8-11 — SATELLITE ENGINE
  1. Set up shared Google credential auth and GEE caching strategy.
  2. Implement S1 + S2 + GEDI + SRTM fusion, Chave equation, and
    sampleRegions-based training.
  3. Implement latest-successful-audit baseline logic for credit calculation.
  4. Implement /audit/result and paginated /audit/history/{land_id}.

WEEKS 12-14 — BLOCKCHAIN + DASHBOARD SUPPORT
  1. Finalize TerraToken.sol, Hardhat tests, and Polygon Amoy deployment.
  2. Implement canonical IPFS metadata generation and minting service.
  3. Implement paginated /credits/balance and optional backend-side balance check.
  4. Validate end-to-end minting and certificate URLs.

WEEKS 15-16 — TESTING, HARDENING, DEPLOYMENT
  1. Enable production rate limiting, structured logging, and backup routines.
  2. Run AR accuracy field tests and GPS zone-arrival validation with the
     mobile team before production sign-off.
  3. Run mock-GPS and backend-only-access-policy validation.
  4. Deploy the single backend web service to Render with Upstash Redis.
  5. Upload backend-google-service-account.json as a Render Secret File and
     verify GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS resolves correctly.
  6. Configure cron-job.org keep-alive/health monitoring.
  7. Run full end-to-end tests, including no-credit completion and recovery paths.

================================================================================
APPENDIX A: ALIGNMENT CLARIFICATIONS (APRIL 2026)
================================================================================

1. Realtime geofencing boundary check
  The mobile app performs local point-in-polygon checks against cached
  official GeoJSON. The backend does not expose a per-GPS realtime endpoint.
  Server-side ST_Contains() remains mandatory during sample submission.

2. Boundary rejection handling
  When the farmer rejects a fetched boundary, the mobile app must preserve
  the OCR-extracted document fields and either retry /land/fetch-boundary
  once or switch to the manual-upload path. The backend should treat both
  paths as the same registration flow and not require a fresh OCR run unless
  the image itself changed.

3. Photo evidence contract
  evidence_photo_base64 must represent the final JPEG bytes only, with no
  data URI prefix. Resize to a maximum of 1280×960 pixels, strip EXIF metadata,
  and compress at JPEG quality 85 before hashing/upload.
  evidence_photo_hash is SHA-256 of those exact decoded bytes. Backend must
  reject mismatches.

4. Species acceptance contract
  The backend accepts only the 11 approved species.
  MODEL_AUTO requires species_confidence >= 0.80.
  MODEL_CONFIRMED requires 0.60 <= species_confidence < 0.80 and an explicit
  farmer confirmation in the mobile flow.
  MANUAL_SELECTED permits lower model confidence only when the farmer chose an
  approved species label explicitly after a low-confidence or NOT_APPROVED
  result. Any unknown species label must still be rejected upstream.

5. GPS quality contract
  Zone arrival requires distance <= 10m and gps_accuracy_m <= 20.
  Backend should reject saved scans with gps_accuracy_m > 30.

6. Rate limiting
  Minimum production limits:
  POST /api/v1/land/verify-document = 10/hour/user
  GET /api/v1/land/fetch-boundary = 20/hour/user
  GET /api/v1/audit/zones = 5/hour/user
  POST /api/v1/audit/submit-samples = 3/hour/user
  GET /api/v1/audit/result/{audit_id} = 60/minute/user
  GET /api/v1/credits/balance = 30/minute/user
  Return HTTP 429 with Retry-After.
  Parcel-scale Earth Engine work runs inside the single fusion Celery task for
  that audit, not separate nested Celery subtasks. MVP quota policy starts
  with Celery concurrency=1 so only one parcel-scale GEE fusion job is active
  per worker/project until quota testing proves a higher safe limit. If Earth
  Engine returns quota or rate-limit errors, keep the audit status at
  CALCULATING, back off, and retry within Celery rather than requiring the
  mobile client to resubmit.

7. Monitoring and backup
  Production services must emit structured JSON logs with audit_id, land_id,
  user_id, route, dependency name, retry count, and latency.
  Celery task failures and blockchain minting failures must trigger alerts.
  Logs must be PII-safe and must not contain plain phone numbers, names,
  Aadhaar, private keys, or raw OCR output.
  Run daily database backups, weekly storage manifest exports, and quarterly
  restore drills.
  Blockchain event history plus IPFS metadata are the source of truth for
  already-issued credits if off-chain recovery is required.

8. Wallet recovery requirement
  Production launch requires an admin-assisted wallet rotation capability.
  The backend must support identity-verified replacement of the farmer wallet
  while preserving future credit issuance and future certificate continuity.
  Already-issued on-chain balances remain at the original wallet in v3.1 and
  must be disclosed clearly in the product copy.

9. Aadhaar handling
  Aadhaar is collected only because OTP proves phone possession, while the
  platform also needs a one-time identity anchor for owner-name matching and
  wallet-recovery re-verification.
  It is not used for biomass calculation, credit minting, or routine app use.
  Plain Aadhaar must be processed only in volatile memory, hashed
  immediately, excluded from logs, and never stored as an image or plaintext.
  Production launch requires legal review against India's Digital Personal
  Data Protection Act, 2023 (DPDPA).

10. Mobile/frontend-safe RPC key separation
  If the React Native 0.86.2 mobile app performs optional direct chain
  validation, it must use a different frontend-safe Alchemy key from the
  backend server key.

11. iOS future scope
  iOS support requires a separate ARKit bridge, non-Android-specific boundary
  map rendering, Apple device attestation/App Check support, Firebase static
  framework configuration in CocoaPods, and Keychain parity testing before it
  can enter scope.

12. Versioning and contract upgrade policy
  Mobile releases use semantic versioning plus platform build numbers.
  Breaking API changes require a new API path version and a minimum supported
  app version gate.
  Minted audits remain historically immutable when xgboost_model_version changes.
  ERC-1155 contract changes are handled via new contract deployment rather than
  in-place mutation of historical token records.

13. Audit due definition
  "Audit due" means once per calendar audit_year.
  If a parcel already has a current-year audit, /audit/zones should return
  HTTP 409 with existing_audit_id and current status so the mobile UI can
  route to resume/status instead of attempting a duplicate audit.

================================================================================
END OF PRESERVED FARMER BASELINE
================================================================================

================================================================================
SECTION 15: v4.0 — DOMAIN ARCHITECTURE AND AUTHENTICATION CONTRACT
================================================================================

15.1 ADDITIVE SERVER ARCHITECTURE
--------------------------------
Purpose:
  Extend the same backend with institution services. Keep the farmer branch intact.
  Institution source is implemented here; see Section 23 for release validation.

Shared infrastructure:
  - Firebase identity verification and FastAPI /api/v1.
  - PostgreSQL, PostGIS and protected Storage.
  - Celery workers and Redis.

Architecture:
  SAME MOBILE APP: common login / UI / AR / API transport
     |
     +-- FARMER BRANCH
     |      -> existing /land, /audit, /credits
     |      -> farmer records and existing credit minting
     |
     +-- INSTITUTION BRANCH
            -> campus, annual audit, accounting and advisory services
            -> institution records, reports and advice
            -> NO minting or retirement

  Farmer tree adapter -----+
                           +-> shared biomass / satellite engine
  Campus tree adapter -----+      -> domain-scoped, versioned result

  Institution Advisor
     -> approved knowledge corpus + scoped immutable relational facts
     -> validated answer

Reuse rules:
  - Share geometry, sampling, allometry, satellite features and model inference.
  - Both domains keep their own routers, records, authorization and persistence.
  - Institution results feed accounting/report/advisor services only.
  - Never retrieve a farmer audit to supply campus removal.

15.2 EXTENDED GET /api/v1/auth/me
--------------------------------
Purpose: Discover the verified user, account domain and institution access.
Auth:    Firebase ID token required.
Header:  Optional X-TerraTrust-Flow: FARMER or INSTITUTION.
Response: Keep the existing flat response; add the institution fields below.

Existing fields — retained:
  - user_id, firebase_uid and phone_number.
  - full_name and kyc_completed.
  - wallet_address.
  - wallet_recovery_status and wallet_recovery_requested_at.

New response fields:
  Field                         | Meaning / values
  ------------------------------|------------------------------------------
  account_domain                | FARMER or INSTITUTION
  role                          | Stored server role; INSTITUTION_ADMIN added
  institution_onboarding_status | REQUIRED, PENDING_REVIEW, REJECTED, COMPLETE
  institution_memberships       | Authorized institution memberships
  capabilities                  | flow/read/registration/advisor booleans (15.4)

Each institution_memberships item:
  Field              | Meaning / values
  -------------------|------------------------------------------
  institution_id     | Authorized institution UUID
  name               | Institution name
  permission         | OWNER, MANAGER, VIEWER
  membership_status  | PENDING, ACTIVE, REVOKED
  institution_status | PENDING, APPROVED, REJECTED, SUSPENDED

FARMER RESPONSE
  - account_domain = FARMER.
  - institution_memberships = [].
  - institution_onboarding_status is nullable.
  - Existing farmer response fields and envelopes remain compatible.

INSTITUTION RESPONSE
  - Farmer-only fields may be NULL or false.
  - These values do not instruct the app to run farmer KYC or wallet setup.
  - INSTITUTION_ADMIN alone grants no access to an institution.
  - Staff role scope remains separate from member permissions.

FIRST PROVISIONING
  Request / identity                  | Server behavior
  ------------------------------------|------------------------------------------
  New UID, no header or FARMER        | Existing minimal farmer provisioning
  New UID, INSTITUTION                | Restricted institution identity
  Existing UID, matching/no header    | Return its stored domain and role
  Existing UID, explicit other domain | Reject mismatch; do not mutate identity

New institution identity:
  - role = INSTITUTION_ADMIN; onboarding = REQUIRED.
  - No membership, Aadhaar/KYC or wallet is created.
  - If institution capability is disabled, return explicit unavailability.
  - Do not provision a farmer as a fallback for an institution request.

CLIENT REQUEST COORDINATION
  Step 1: Record the pending login flow before phone verification.
  Step 2: Use one coordinator for LoginScreen and the root auth observer.
  Step 3: Keep the same flow header until account_domain is confirmed.
  Step 4: Resume interrupted sign-in with its pending flow intent.
  Step 5: Restore ordinary returning sessions from their stored server domain.

  Rule: A headerless farmer-default call must not race institution provisioning.

SERVER VALIDATION
  - Verify the Firebase token before accepting login intent.
  - Treat the header as an intent hint; it never grants a role or membership.
  - Allow the header in transport and any configured CORS allowlist.
  - Enforce unique Firebase UID and transactional upsert.
  - Keep the account domain immutable in the initial release.
  - Backfill existing nonstaff users as FARMER; preserve inspected staff roles.
  - Do not add a single users.institution_id field.

INSTITUTION ACCESS
  - Business writes require ACTIVE membership, APPROVED tenant and capability.
  - Released reads and own privacy/status follow feature-off rules in 15.4.
  - Pending creators may use own setup/evidence/status and account privacy only.
  - Onboarding state or role alone cannot unlock a tenant.

Onboarding state:
  Condition                          | Returned state
  -----------------------------------|--------------------
  Approved setup/accepted membership | COMPLETE
  Own registration awaiting review   | PENDING_REVIEW
  Own registration returned/rejected | REJECTED + reason
  No completed/pending registration  | REQUIRED

  Completed onboarding stays COMPLETE after later revocation or suspension.
  Operational access remains blocked until ACTIVE/APPROVED membership returns.

Errors:
  400 INVALID_LOGIN_FLOW:
    Unknown header value.
  409 ACCOUNT_DOMAIN_MISMATCH:
    Return expected_domain and selected_domain. Do not change the user row.

15.3 PERMISSION POLICY
----------------------

OWNER
-----

  - profile/campus/draft/survey/audit/advice plus members.

MANAGER
-------

  - same operational write rights excluding ownership/member administration.

VIEWER
------

  - finalized results/authorized maps/reports/published finalized-context history;
    no drafts/new advice.
PLATFORM REVIEWER
------------------

  - Operator reviewer capability is independently assigned to tenant/case; can
    review authority/boundary/mask/plots/surveys and finalize audit after approval
    checks.
COMMON ACCESS RULES
--------------------

  - If existing ADMIN/AUDITOR are available, map explicitly after backend
    inspection.
  - No institution self-approval; reviewer/finalizer independence is defined against
    membership and contributors in 15.4/19.1.
  - A last active owner cannot be revoked/demoted.
  - No invitation to arbitrary institution via payload.
  - OWNER/MANAGER may create calculation/export/advice jobs; VIEWER only reads
    already-generated authorized finalized exports/results.
  - Registration creation is limited to own restricted institution identity;
    invitation acceptance adds only its reviewed membership.
  - Core setup allows one pending registration per UID; joining more institutions
    requires reviewed invitations.
  - Existing-owner new-tenant provisioning is operator-controlled, not an
    unrestricted create call.
  - Institution/member suspension or revocation invalidates writes/download
    issuing/advice and job publication; minimal access-status view remains for own
    identity.

15.4 IDENTITY, REGISTRATION AND MEMBERSHIP COMPLETION
-----------------------------------------------------
Contract precedence:
  - Sections 15.4, 16.0–16.3 and 18.6–18.11 make field/route summaries concrete.
  - They apply to new institution contracts only.
  - An example or short Purpose line never overrides the typed contract.
  - /auth/me remains the flat legacy response in 15.2; other new routes use 18.6.

Identity verification:
  - Use Firebase Admin ID-token verification with configured project issuer,
    audience, signature, expiry and revocation/disabled-user checks.
  - Do not accept a custom token, phone number, payload UID or selector as identity.
  - Check current database membership and operator scope on each request.
  - An institution domain stays immutable for that Firebase UID.
  - Existing staff roles are preserved; only explicit operator grants authorize
    staff work.
  - A staff user does not obtain mobile tenant access automatically.

Additional /auth/me fields — additive and server-owned:
  registration_id              : UUID|null, own current registration revision.
  registration_institution_id  : UUID|null, owning institution navigation pointer.
  institution_offline_context  : {checked_at,expires_at,context_hash}|null;
    server-issued <=24h profile allowance for confirmed institution sessions.
    Capture additionally requires the exact campus/survey grant in 18.10.
  registration_authorizations  : {id,expires_at}[] own usable operator grants.
  institution_links            : {privacy,support,account_deletion} HTTPS URLs.
  These pointers/links grant no tenant permissions.
  registration_business_status : RegistrationStatus|null; see 16.2.
  capabilities                 : Object with booleans below.
    institution_flow           : Institution mutation feature enabled.
    institution_read           : Authorized institution read branch available.
    institution_registration   : New registration/invite operations enabled.
    institution_advisor        : New advice/scenario feature available.

Onboarding precedence:
  - A user who completed onboarding keeps COMPLETE after revocation/suspension.
  - Operational access still requires current APPROVED tenant + ACTIVE membership.
  - Otherwise, active approved membership -> COMPLETE.
  - Submitted/under-review own registration -> PENDING_REVIEW.
  - Returned/rejected own registration -> REJECTED, with reason.
  - No registration, or an unsubmitted own draft -> REQUIRED.
  - REQUIRED with registration_id resumes that draft; it does not create a new one.

Registration states and ownership:
  - InstitutionSetupScreen initially saves locally under the authenticated UID.
  - POST /institutions creates PENDING institution, pending OWNER membership and
    registration revision 1 in DRAFT.
  - It does not submit for review.
  - Before that POST, evidence parent REGISTRATION_DRAFT uses parent_id=users.id.
  - The backend owns this namespace; no separate draft-create API is required.
  - POST binds READY evidence owned by that UID to the new registration.
  - Change its initial parent to REGISTRATION/new registration ID and set
    institution_id in that transaction; retain immutable object bytes/key/hash.
  - Create checked evidence-link rows.
  - Only this initial unfrozen binding may change the pretenant parent; later
    corrections add links, never reparent.
  - Submit freezes its input_revision/hash, sets SUBMITTED and creates a review case.
  - Claim/start review -> UNDER_REVIEW.
  - APPROVE -> registration APPROVED, institution APPROVED and creator OWNER ACTIVE
    in one transaction.
  - RETURN -> NEEDS_CORRECTION. REJECT -> REJECTED. Both preserve frozen evidence.
  - Correction creates a new registration UUID/revision and DRAFT snapshot.
  - PATCH may update only that unfrozen draft; resubmit creates a new case.
  - One open registration family per creator UID; duplicate submission returns its
    existing ID for the same key/hash, or 409 for conflicting creation.
  - Existing approved owners create additional institutions only through an
    operator-authorized registration; never through an unrestricted mobile call.
  - Duplicate legal/control evidence is an operator-review issue, not a name-only
    automatic rejection or proof of authority.

Invitations:
  - OWNER of an APPROVED institution invites a verified E.164 phone identity.
  - POST /members creates an invitation, not an ACTIVE membership.
  - Generate at least 256 bits of random token entropy; store SHA-256 in the invitation row; bounded encrypted receipt is the 19.2 exception.
  - Initial expiry: 7 days. Owner can revoke/reissue; reissue invalidates old token.
  - Return invitation_token once for explicit copy/share and recipient paste.
  - Initial invitation_url=NULL; no automatic email/SMS or token-link handler.
  - Reissue uses the atomic endpoint in 18.11 and invalidates the old token.
  - Acceptance requires Firebase phone identity to match the invitation target,
    INSTITUTION domain, unexpired/unrevoked token and APPROVED institution.
  - Successful acceptance creates/activates only its specified membership and
    returns COMPLETE.
  - There is no second authority review for an approved tenant.
  - An already ACTIVE member cannot use an old invitation to change permission.
  - OWNER must use the membership permission operation instead.
  - Accepted-token retry by the same target returns the original membership; other
    targets receive a generic invalid-invitation error.
  - Raw tokens never appear in logs, analytics, query history or notifications.
  - InstitutionSetupScreen has an invitation-only mode for both new and existing
    institution identities; it never edits an unrelated registration.

Permission/lifecycle rules:
  - {uid} in /members/{uid} means the internal users.id UUID, not Firebase UID.
  - OWNER can set OWNER/MANAGER/VIEWER or revoke ACTIVE/PENDING membership.
  - Lock institution and membership rows before last-OWNER checks.
  - Last active OWNER must transfer ownership before leaving/deletion; no orphan.
  - Reviewer cannot be a tenant member or creator/contributor/submitter of the case.
  - Enforce this in the backend, independently of hidden mobile buttons.
  - VIEWER can read FINALIZED and previously finalized SUPERSEDED revisions;
    'finalized-only' shorthand throughout the documents includes both.
  - VIEWER campus maps/tree results are limited to accepted snapshots linked to an
    authorized finalized/superseded audit, not current operational drafts.
  - OWNER/MANAGER can read their tenant's operational drafts and accepted history.
  - Advice is tenant-shared among OWNER/MANAGER; VIEWER reads only PUBLISHED advice
    whose original context was finalized.
  - Supersession adds a historical badge.

OTP purpose and reauthentication:
  - Shared coordinator records purpose SIGN_IN or REAUTHENTICATE.
  - Only a fresh SIGN_IN creates demo login_session_id/reset handoff.
  - REAUTHENTICATE verifies the SAME existing UID for privacy/ownership actions,
    preserves domain/tenant/ demo generation and does not reset a checkpoint.
  - Token refresh is not sign-in.
  - Cancel/UID change invalidates callbacks.
  - Reauthentication cannot switch domain, create membership, clear unfinished work
    or run farmer wallet effects.

Feature-off behavior:
  - New institution provisioning/registration while disabled -> 503
    INSTITUTION_FLOW_DISABLED, retryable=false; never create a farmer fallback.
  - Existing identity still receives its true domain and memberships.
  - With institution_flow=false, approved members may open a read-only branch for
    finalized/superseded results, exports and published historical advice.
  - Disable writes, new jobs and new advice; return 503 on those API attempts.
  - Suspended/revoked access remains restricted regardless of read capability.
  - Advisor off blocks new asks/scenarios, but authorized history remains readable.
  - App-wide maintenance may block all branches using the existing maintenance flow.

================================================================================

SECTION 16: v4.0 — ADDITIVE DATA MODEL AND INVARIANTS
================================================================================

16.0 DATABASE TABLE FORMAT AND SHARED COLUMNS
--------------------------------------------
Purpose: Define the exact PostgreSQL columns used by the institution flow.

How to read a table:
  Column          : The physical PostgreSQL column name.
  PostgreSQL type : The full storage type. Named statuses are TEXT with a CHECK.
  NULL allowed    : Yes means the value may be unknown or not yet applicable.
  Rule / default  : Required default, reference or field-specific restriction.

Common columns — every NEW table includes these:

  Column         | PostgreSQL type | NULL allowed | Rule / default
  ---------------|-----------------|--------------|-------------------------------
  id             | UUID            | No           | PK; gen_random_uuid()
  created_at     | TIMESTAMPTZ     | No           | now(); server receipt time
  updated_at     | TIMESTAMPTZ     | No           | now(); maintained by trigger
  input_revision | INTEGER         | No           | DEFAULT 1; CHECK > 0

Institution ownership:
  - Institution-owned tables also contain institution_id UUID NOT NULL.
  - institution_id references institutions.id.
  - Add UNIQUE(institution_id,id) for composite parent/child foreign keys.
  - Institutions is the root table; it has no institution_id self-reference.
  - Global and user-owned exceptions are identified on their table cards.
  - An explicitly nullable institution_id in a table card overrides the shared rule.

Actors and provenance:
  - User/actor columns reference users.id, not the Firebase UID.
  - Member/operator-created resources record created_by unless their card names
    author_id, actor_id or requested_by for that responsibility.
  - System work uses requested_by or actor_id as specified on its table card.
  - Tenant business resources, artifacts and registries include data_origin TEXT.
  - Default: LIVE.
  - Allowed values: LIVE or SYNTHETIC.
  - Production rejects SYNTHETIC for new institution work/accepted output.
  - UUID arrays default to [] unless the selected method requires a nonempty list.
  - Validate every referenced ID in the same write transaction.

Numeric values and structured data:
  - Use NUMERIC(24,12) for activity, carbon, area and other ledger quantities.
  - Unknown values are NULL. Do not give them an implicit zero default.
  - SHA-256 values use lowercase 64-character hexadecimal TEXT with a CHECK.
  - JSONB follows the named request/configuration schema in Section 18.
  - schema_version defaults to 1; the serializer supplies it when it is not a column.
  - Frozen business data cannot be overwritten; lifecycle changes follow Section 19.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

TABLE: users
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Purpose: Adds institution account-domain and onboarding fields to the existing user.

Scope:   Existing shared table; institution additions only

Columns:

  Column                              | PostgreSQL type | NULL allowed | Rule / default
  ------------------------------------|-----------------|--------------|-------------------------------
  account_domain                      | TEXT            | No           | DEFAULT FARMER CHECK FARMER, INSTITUTION
  institution_onboarding_status       | TEXT            | Yes          | CHECK REQUIRED, PENDING_REVIEW, REJECTED, COMPLETE
  institution_onboarding_completed_at | TIMESTAMPTZ     | Yes          | —

Validation and storage rules:
  - Extend existing role CHECK to allow INSTITUTION_ADMIN
  - Keep all existing user IDs/roles/Firebase UID uniqueness.
  - Farmer-only Aadhaar/KYC/wallet requirements apply to FARMER only
  - Institutions get no fabricated Aadhaar/wallet.
  - Do not add users.institution_id.

  review_reason           | TEXT                     | Yes          | —
  archived_at             | TIMESTAMPTZ              | Yes          | —

Validation and storage rules:
  - ProfileData holds name/type/address/contact/timezone/convention/counts/area.
  - API flattened summary name is profile.name, not a second mutable source.

TABLE: campus_tree_measurements
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Purpose: Stores one tree observation, its dimensions, sources and evidence.

Scope:   Institution-owned
Shared columns: id, created_at, updated_at, input_revision (16.0).

Columns:

  Column                 | PostgreSQL type      | NULL allowed     | Rule / default
  -----------------------|----------------------|------------------|-------------------------------
  campus_id              | UUID                 | No               | —
  survey_id              | UUID                 | No               | —
  tree_id                | UUID                 | No               | —
  plot_id                | UUID                 | No               | —
  local_uuid             | UUID                 | No               | —
  revision               | INTEGER              | No               | DEFAULT 1
  measured_at            | TIMESTAMPTZ          | No               | —
  location               | geometry(Point,4326) | Yes              | —
  gps_accuracy_m         | NUMERIC(24,12)       | Yes              | —
  dbh_cm                 | NUMERIC(24,12)       | Yes              | —
  height_m               | NUMERIC(24,12)       | Yes              | —
  height_source          | TEXT                 | No               | —
  species_code           | TEXT                 | Yes              | —
  species_selection      | TEXT                 | No               | —
  species_confidence     | NUMERIC(24,12)       | Yes              | —
  ar_tier                | INTEGER              | No               | —
  allometry_id           | UUID                 | Yes              | —
  wood_density_source_id | UUID                 | Yes              | —
  measurement_height_m   | NUMERIC(24,12)       | No               | —
  protocol_version       | UUID                 | No               | —
  measurement_form       | TEXT                 | No               | —
  circumference_cm       | NUMERIC(24,12)       | Yes              | —
  stems                  | JSONB                | No               | —
  evidence_ids           | UUID[]               | No               | —
  capture_quality        | JSONB                | No               | Schema CaptureQuality
  content_hash           | TEXT (SHA-256)       | No               | MCJ1 of raw write item
  raw_input              | JSONB                | Yes, legacy only | Exact raw item; mandatory on every new capture/write (21.7)
  derived_dbh_cm         | NUMERIC(24,12)       | Yes              | Reviewed server derivation, never overwrites raw DBH
  validation_status      | TEXT                 | No               | CHECK VALID, NEEDS_DATA
  validation_issues      | JSONB                | No               | —
  created_by             | UUID                 | No               | —
  copied_from_id         | UUID                 | Yes              | -> campus_tree_measurements.id
  deleted_at             | TIMESTAMPTZ          | Yes              | —

Validation and storage rules:
  - UNIQUE(institution_id,local_uuid)
  - one nondeleted observation per (survey_id,tree_id). content_hash is the
    acknowledged client_content_hash
  - Immutable source density, allometry/value/unit/provenance are resolved into the
    frozen survey snapshot.
  - GET remaps raw_input keys plus content_hash to the write-item/hash fields.
  - Never replace raw dbh_cm/Decimal spelling with a server derivation/quantization.
    dbh_cm remains raw input; derived_dbh_cm/validation are separate read fields.
  - base_survey_revision stays local.

  reviewed_at          | TIMESTAMPTZ             | Yes          | —
  finalized_at         | TIMESTAMPTZ             | Yes          | —
  latest_review_reason | TEXT                    | Yes          | —

Validation and storage rules:
  - UNIQUE(logical_audit_id,revision)
  - Partial UNIQUE family for DRAFT/SUBMITTED/UNDER_REVIEW; separate FINALIZED index.
  - active_draft_audit_id points to that working revision even while frozen in review;
    clear on RETURN/finalize, replace only through an authorized correction.
  - Period/timezone copies must equal family.
  - PATCH dates/timezone allowed only for the first unsubmitted family revision
  - update family and child under tenant lock.
