Package rule:
  - Add __init__.py to the new parent package directories models, repositories,
    services, scripts and demo if the baseline has not already made them packages.
  - Existing deployment entry and Celery app stay the baseline ones in Section 3.
  - Register institution task modules with that app; use isolated queues in 21.6.
  - Research artifacts in SRS 33.1 are evaluation deliverables. They are not runtime
    imports or a source of unreviewed model defaults.

REQUEST IMPLEMENTATION ORDER
----------------------------
  1. main.py mounts each router once with /api/v1. Router paths omit that prefix.
     The route cards in 18 show complete public paths; never produce /api/v1 twice.
  2. dependencies.py verifies the Firebase token using the baseline verifier.
     institution_access.py loads domain, membership/operator scope and parent chain.
     A Firebase UID alone never supplies a tenant, permission or operator grant.
  3. Parse the exact DTO from 18.6/18.9/18.11; reject unknown writable fields.
     Convert Decimal values at the boundary and serialize them as decimal strings.
  4. Open one SQL transaction through the existing server DB connection. Pass that
     connection plus verified actor context to the service and repositories.
     Do not emulate a transaction using separate Supabase REST calls.
  5. Authorize the current parent before looking up an idempotent response. On an
     authorized replay return its saved receipt before checking stale If-Match.
  6. Lock rows in tenant -> family/campus -> resource -> case/job order. Validate
     state, original counter, source applicability and fixture generation.
  7. Commit mutation, new counter/hash, event, notification, job/outbox and receipt
     together when that operation requires them. Roll back all on failure.
  8. Return the public model, never the raw DB row. The institution error handler
     translates typed failures only for institution routes; preserve farmer errors.
  9. Workers reload the frozen input and authorization/generation before publish.
     Retry outbox delivery; never retry an external side effect by creating a job.

DOMAIN IMPLEMENTATION MAP
-------------------------
All paths below are relative to their named folders above. Full columns: 16.0.

Flow              | Router / DTO module              | Service / repository module
------------------|----------------------------------|---------------------------------
Setup/members     | institutions / institutions      | institution_service / institutions
Campus/geometry   | campuses / campuses              | campus_service / campuses
Trees/surveys     | campus_trees / trees + surveys    | survey_service / trees
Stock processing  | campus_trees / surveys           | campus_tree_adapter / trees
Annual inputs     | institution_audits / accounting  | accounting_service / accounting
CSV               | institution_audits / accounting  | import_service / accounting
Review/finalize   | institution_admin / reviews      | workflow_service / reviews
Reports/trends    | institution_reports / accounting | report_service + analytics_service / accounting
Evidence          | institution_evidence / evidence  | shared.evidence_service / evidence
Registry/source   | institution_registries / registries | factor_service + ingestion_service / registries
Advice/scenarios  | advisory / advisory              | advisory_service + scenario_service / advisory
Jobs              | institution_jobs / jobs          | tasks.institution_tasks / jobs
Inbox/privacy     | institution_account / privacy    | notification_service + privacy_service / privacy
Demo handshake    | institutions / demo              | demo.institution.sessions + restore / demo

Table and worker ownership:
  - Setup owns institutions, registrations, memberships, invitations and grants.
  - Campus owns campuses, controls, boundaries, masks, plot sets and plots.
    campus_mask and campus_plot_generation publish their separate versioned outputs.
  - Trees owns trees/events, surveys, measurements and campus tree audits.
    campus_fusion reads campus-owned snapshots; no farmer land/audit persistence.
  - Accounting owns institution audits, input lines, links, imports, calculation
    snapshots/lines and final results. institution_calculation publishes by hash.
  - Reports owns exports/artifacts; report_export reads the selected frozen result.
  - Evidence owns evidence/reference links and private object lifecycle.
  - Registry owns factors/methods/models/quality/knowledge/corpus versions.
    knowledge_ingestion/reindex cannot activate an unreviewed source.
  - Advice owns queries, retrieval records, recommendations, scenarios and feedback.
    advisory worker cannot change accounting or accepted campus observations.
  - Shared jobs, outbox, receipts, events and review cases use the same transaction
    and parent authorization as the domain row. Names in 16.0 are physical SQL names.

REUSE BOUNDARIES
----------------
  - Reuse Firebase verification, API bootstrap, DB/storage clients and worker app.
  - Reuse raw AR/GPS/species and satellite acquisition through domain-neutral input.
    Preserve farmer outputs, defaults, tasks, names and side effects.
  - If a shared helper accepts land_id or farmer audit rows, wrap its pure operation
    through campus_tree_adapter.py. Do not fabricate a farmer record to call it.
  - New campus scientific eligibility/rounding follows 17, never farmer fallbacks.
  - Institution accounting and reports have no wallet, KYC, mint or Pinata dependency.
  - Export/advisor failure cannot change accepted deterministic result values.

19.1 EXACT REVIEW, FINALIZATION AND CAMPUS STATE TRANSITIONS
-------------------------------------------------------------
Required operator interface:
  - Implement authenticated internal review tooling backed by the review-case APIs.
  - A private admin console or internal API client suffices; no second mobile app.
  - It must list assigned cases, claim them, inspect frozen data/evidence/issues,
    enter reasons and issue the applicable decision.
  - A list of POST routes alone is not a complete review workflow.
  - Review case input_revision starts at 1 and increments on claim/renew/release and
    decision; wire name case_input_revision distinguishes resource version.
  - A review case claim lasts 30 minutes and can be renewed by the scoped reviewer.
  - Expiry permits another authorized claim; it does not auto-approve or return
    data.
  - START, RETURN, APPROVE, ACCEPT and finalize require exact case/hash/revision.
  - Freeze contributor_user_ids in review_cases.resource_snapshot at case creation:
    creator, submitter, input/evidence editors, source authors and provider requester
    IDs from the reviewed revision's audit_events/source lineage.
  - Compare reviewer/finalizer to that frozen set and historical tenant memberships
    covering the reviewed work. Revoking/demoting oneself cannot remove a conflict.
    Source author/reviewer independence also applies to global registries.
  - Membership changes append audit_events.details with target_user_id,
    previous_permission/previous_status, next_permission/next_status and server
    effective_at in the SAME locked transaction. Retain prior intervals when a
    membership is revoked/reactivated; current status alone cannot prove history.
  - Check current scoped grant/expiry and conflicts again at decision/finalize.
  - A replacement control/geometry snapshot closes obsolete open cases with no
    fabricated decision, reason INPUT_SUPERSEDED and a system audit event. Keep old
    snapshot/claim/decision history; stale approvals return 409 CASE_SUPERSEDED.

Registration:
  DRAFT -> SUBMITTED -> UNDER_REVIEW -> APPROVED or NEEDS_CORRECTION/REJECTED.
  - Institution verification remains PENDING until APPROVE; pending OWNER cannot use
    campus jobs.
  - Submission alone never activates membership.
  - REJECT sets tenant REJECTED. A corrected registration resets tenant to PENDING.
  - SUSPEND/RESUME use scoped operator verification with reason after approval.
  - RESUME is explicit; never silently re-enable from a client refresh.

Campus control and geometry:
  - Create campus -> control PENDING. Boundary POST -> PENDING_REVIEW.
  - CAMPUS_CONTROL APPROVE verifies exact frozen control evidence and sets control
    APPROVED/control_approved_hash through 18.11 control-review.
  - Boundary APPROVE requires that approved control hash, validates geometry and
    sets current_boundary_id.
  - RETURN leaves the boundary unapproved.
  - Mask/plots processing SUCCEEDED produces a candidate, not an approved resource.
  - Review candidate mask/plot-set -> APPROVED or NEEDS_CORRECTION.
  - current_mask_id/current_plot_set_id change only on compatible approval.
  - Approving a new boundary invalidates current operational mask/plot pointers
    unless they explicitly reference that boundary; old accepted snapshots stay.
  - Returned mask/plots create a new generation with correction_of ID; never edit an
    artifact used by an accepted survey/result.
  - Archive campus blocks new work.
  - Existing finalized references remain readable; restore requires OWNER/MANAGER
    action, sets control PENDING and requires fresh control/boundary review before
    new work.

Survey and biomass:
  - Submit checks geometry/policy/plot support, one observation per tree and READY
    evidence.
  - Freeze input_revision/hash and create its review case.
  - ACCEPT makes the survey eligible for the biomass job; RETURN -> NEEDS_CORRECTION.
  - New survey revision clones selected data into a draft with new survey_id.
  - Biomass job success requires a separate result/comparability review before
    linking to an annual audit.
  - SUCCEEDED is not equivalent to ACCEPTED.
  - Correcting a tree assessment creates a new tree audit with correction_of ID;
    accepted result revisions remain immutable and noncontributing alternatives.

Annual audit:
  - Submit may include proposed exclusions.
  - Validate all included lines, freeze inputs, set SUBMITTED and start/reuse
    exact-hash calculation if necessary.
  - Exclusions are decided during independent review; they are not magically
    'already reviewed' before the audit can enter review.
  - START -> UNDER_REVIEW. Calculation/evidence must be ready for APPROVE.
  - APPROVE records approved input_hash/result_hash/snapshot_id and coverage.
  - Business status stays UNDER_REVIEW; case stays CLAIMED with decision APPROVE
    until finalize.
  - Claim expiry needs a fresh authorized claim/inspection, not automatic
    publication.
  - Finalize closes the case; RETURN closes for correction.
  - RETURN -> NEEDS_CORRECTION with per-field/source issues.
  - Correction -> new audit_id/revision DRAFT; old frozen revision cannot be
    patched.
  - Finalize requires current approval hashes and independent operator permission.
  - APPROVE accepts the ENTIRE frozen proposed boundary/exclusion set and its exact
    calculation. Rejecting any exclusion requires RETURN and a new corrected draft;
    a reviewer never edits the frozen coverage/numeric rows while approving.
  - Preview totals may quantify the explicitly stated PROPOSED boundary with a
    draft/proposed-exclusion label. Missing included data still yields NULL; no
    released total or full-coverage claim exists before independent finalization.
  - Releasing/reassigning a claim clears its live APPROVE/approved_snapshot_id and
    audit reviewed pointer, preserving the old attestation in audit_events. A new
    claimant inspects and approves again; frozen numerical content is unchanged.
  - Lock period-family row, mark the previous FINALIZED row SUPERSEDED first,
    publish the replacement FINALIZED/result and swap the current-final pointer in
    the SAME transaction. Any failure rolls back both statuses and preserves old final.
  - Submission/calculation failure leaves the revision frozen with an error; retry
    unchanged transient work or RETURN it for data correction.
  - Do not reopen silently.
  - At most one working DRAFT/SUBMITTED/UNDER_REVIEW revision and one current final
    per family. Reject competing correction/submit with 409 REVIEW_IN_PROGRESS.
    NEEDS_CORRECTION remains frozen history; its new DRAFT becomes the working row.
  - Apply the equivalent one-working-revision index to survey/registration families
    using DRAFT/SUBMITTED/UNDER_REVIEW; accepted/returned history stays immutable.

Preview versus result identity:
  - Each calculation creates immutable snapshot_id bound to audit/input hash.
  - Calculate accepts DRAFT or an unchanged SUBMITTED/UNDER_REVIEW frozen hash;
    authorized current OWNER/MANAGER can recover blocked technical processing.
  - It cannot edit frozen inputs or recalculate FINALIZED/SUPERSEDED in place.
  - Calculation previews do NOT insert/overwrite the unique annual final result.
  - GET result?snapshot_id=... may return an authorized draft preview with watermark.
  - Finalize writes institution_audit_results for that audit revision once and binds
    reviewed_snapshot_id.
  - Historical previews remain separately identifiable.
  - ExportCreate uses snapshot_id + result_hash; draft export is supported without
    inventing a FINALIZED row.
  - VIEWER cannot access that draft artifact.

19.2 DURABLE JOBS, RETRY, POLLING AND PUBLICATION
------------------------------------------------
Acceptance:
  - In one transaction save resource/input snapshot, processing_job, idempotency
    acceptance and job_outbox row.
  - Return 202 only after commit.
  - Dispatcher retries publishing pending outbox rows to Redis/Celery.
  - Broker downtime cannot lose accepted work or leave an invisible unqueued
    resource.
  - Worker delivery is at-least-once; a DB stage lease and unique output hash make
    each stage idempotent.
  - Do not claim exactly-once message delivery.

Worker contract:
  - QUEUED -> RUNNING -> SUCCEEDED/FAILED/NEEDS_DATA/BLOCKED_ACCESS/CANCELLED.
  - Recheck access at start and before publishing; revoked/suspended caller ->
    BLOCKED_ACCESS, no new exposed artifacts.
  - Existing published results are retained.
  - Persist stage/checkpoint/provider-task ID, heartbeat, attempts and error.
  - Before provider submission persist deterministic external operation key derived
    from job_id/stage/attempt-independent input hash. Use provider idempotency or
    searchable operation labels and persist the returned task ID immediately.
  - Crash after provider acceptance but before task-ID persistence is an UNKNOWN
    external outcome. Reconcile by that operation key before any resubmission.
    If provider lookup cannot prove the result, mark FAILED/retryable=false with
    EXTERNAL_OUTCOME_UNKNOWN; no automatic new job/key for that unresolved scope.
  - Operations owner records provider lookup, resulting task ID or confirmed absence
    in the existing stage checkpoint and audit event under the job lock. Only then
    may the same job resume within its original budget. No fabricated success.
  - Output remains staged/private until committed with matching input hash.
  - A watchdog detects stale leases and requeues bounded work or marks FAILED.
  - Celery acknowledgement settings alone are not a crash-recovery guarantee.

Retry rules:
  - Transient timeout/429/5xx/broker error may retry with exponential backoff/jitter.
  - Initial automatic retry budget: 3 retries, respecting provider Retry-After.
  - Missing factor/species/imagery support or schema mismatch -> NEEDS_DATA, no
    blind retry.
  - Correct inputs and create a new draft/assessment revision instead.
  - Manual POST /jobs/{jid}/retry requeues the same job/hash only for FAILED with
    retryable=true, attempts remaining and fresh permission/config checks.
  - max_attempts=1+INSTITUTION_JOB_MAX_RETRIES is frozen at acceptance (default 4).
    attempt starts 0 and increments once under lock when a new execution is leased.
    Stages/provider polls/duplicate deliveries do not increment it. Retry POST marks
    QUEUED; it does not increment again or reset the exhausted budget.
  - Frozen deadline is absolute from initial acceptance; retry does not extend it.
    Exhaustion/deadline -> FAILED, retryable=false; a new key cannot bypass this cap.
  - Live/running/succeeded/stale-input retries -> 409 JOB_NOT_RETRIABLE.
  - New idempotency keys do not bypass the unique active job for resource/hash/type.

Polling:
  - Mobile starts at poll_after_seconds=3; backs off to 30s with jitter.
  - Pause when app backgrounds or screen/session owner changes; fetch once on focus.
  - Honor 429 Retry-After; stop on terminal status. Navigation does not cancel work.
  - Job errors include safe code/message/retryable/correction target, not stack traces.

Hash construction:
  - input_hash covers sorted normalized source rows/UUIDs, saved factor/method/model
    versions, evidence byte hashes, geometry lineage, period/coverage/denominators.
  - Encode UTF-8 canonical JSON with sorted keys, Decimal strings and UTC times;
    exclude transport request IDs, signed URLs, job timings and PDF-render bytes.
  - Resolve and freeze all factor/config/artifact references at acceptance; a worker
    never consults a floating latest registry while calculating an accepted
    snapshot.
  - result_hash excludes generated snapshot/result/calculation-line IDs; source
    input/method/model/factor identities remain included.
  - It covers input_hash plus sorted deterministic output values/units, applied
    factor/model/method snapshots, coverage and scientific quality.
  - Lifecycle/reviewer/job timestamps, approval/permission state, preview/export
    watermarks and publication status are separate metadata, excluded from result
    hash.
  - Finalization does not mutate numerical snapshot/hash to remove a watermark.
  - Preserve source model outputs before rendering; repeated exports reuse the exact
    result hash.
  - GET mutable resource returns input_hash for CalculateRequest/SubmitRequest.
  - Resolve unique applicable factor/version pins during explicit draft create/save
    and store them with the acknowledged rows. input_hash is computed afterward.
    Ambiguous factor choices require an explicit approved selection; do not resolve
    by "latest" inside Calculate or a worker. Missing pins remain visible NEEDS_DATA.
  - Calculate/submit reuse that saved source hash and pins; a changed default requires
    an explicit draft write/new input_revision, never a silent hash replacement.
  - Frozen resource input_revision counts content edits and stays fixed after submit.
    START/APPROVE/job progress/finalize update lifecycle metadata and case counters,
    not the frozen input counter/hash. Case claims/decisions increment the separate
    case_input_revision. Generic updated_at is not an input revision trigger.
  - Expected-hash mismatch -> 409 INPUT_HASH_CHANGED, refresh and review before
    retry.
  - Missing factor is represented explicitly in a provisional snapshot, not invented.

Idempotency and stale preconditions:
  - Scope key by verified user + tenant/user scope + generation + method + route + key.
  - normalized_route is the concrete /api/v1 path including canonical resource IDs,
    not a route template; reject encoded separators/ambiguous duplicate slashes.
  - request_hash = SHA256(MCJ1({method,route,query,body,if_match,fixture_generation})).
    method is uppercase; route is that concrete path; query is a sorted-key object
    of allowed string-value arrays; body is the supplied validated JSON or NULL;
    if_match is the parsed integer or NULL; generation is the verified integer/NULL.
    Preserve optional-field omission; do not add resolved/default/source values.
    Use {} for absent query. Scope already binds the verified user and tenant.
  - Store that canonical request hash; same key/different hash -> 409.
  - Authorize first, then resolve accepted identical retry, then check If-Match for
    a genuinely new mutation.
  - This avoids rejecting a successful lost-response retry.
  - Detailed receipts last at least 72h; expires_at is the compaction deadline,
    not permission to execute that key again. Retain a TOMBSTONE with scope, request
    hash, original status/resource ID/revision/recovery kind and no secret body.
  - Same request after compaction -> 409 IDEMPOTENCY_RECEIPT_EXPIRED with safe own
    resource reference in Error.details. Never treat the old key as a fresh write.
    Return mutation_committed=true, original accepted_revision and recovery_kind
    with the two existing safe recovery fields; match scope/body/counter first.
    That trusted tombstone acknowledges the original committed operation even for a
    DELETE whose row no longer exists; it never means the mutation executed again.
    RESOURCE_GET refreshes current authorized state; DELETE_ACK/ACK_ONLY can mark
    the original operation ACKED directly. SECRET_REISSUE requires explicit owner
    reissue to recover a lost code. Never silently choose a new mutation key.
  - Take a transaction-scoped advisory lock derived from the complete receipt scope
    BEFORE receipt lookup or business-state validation, then lock tenant/family/
    resource rows in the established order. Recheck access at commit.
    Same-key losers read the winner's committed receipt; rollback permits a later
    genuine acceptance. Keep the unique index as an independent integrity guard.
    Do not call a provider before this transaction commits.
  - Include the original If-Match/body counter and business query filters in the
    request hash. Exclude bearer token, request ID and newly minted signed URL.
    Validate body shape before comparison; state/source applicability follows replay.
  - For resettable demos, verify current fixture_generation BEFORE lookup/replay and
    include it in the unique scope. Session handshake uses login_session_id instead.
  - Invitation tokens are hashed in invitations. Receipt.response excludes raw token;
    only secret_ciphertext stores its bounded encrypted replay value. Local AES key fingerprint
    and expiry are required; no token in JSON logs/backups outside encryption.
    Same authorized original caller may decrypt during TTL; after it, return the
    expired receipt error and require explicit reissue/revocation if a code was lost.
    A receipt is the sole bounded exception to storing invitation hashes only.
  - Missing required If-Match -> 428; wrong/stale value -> 409 with safe own current
    revision.
  - HTTP If-Match uses quoted integer ETag, for example "7".
  - Client-operation hashes exclude transport headers/random request_id; they
    include canonical body, resource scope and intended revision.

Blocked-access recovery:
  - BLOCKED_ACCESS is not an automatic/manual retry of an obsolete actor's request.
  - A currently authorized member submits a new request/job bound to their identity.
  - Completed immutable input/stage artifacts may be reused after fresh permission,
    lineage and hash checks; no revival of a revoked actor's authorization.

19.3 CONTROLLED OPERATOR PROVISIONING
-------------------------------------
Purpose: Break the initial reviewer-creation dependency without granting app users
administration privileges. This is a deployment command, not a public endpoint.

File: scripts/institution/bootstrap_operator.py; use the baseline Python env.
  python -m scripts.institution.bootstrap_operator --firebase-uid <verified-uid>
    --capability <name-from-operator_grants-in-16.0> --institution-id <authorized-uuid>
    --expires-at <RFC3339-UTC> --reason <change-record-id> --dry-run
  Replace --dry-run with --apply after reviewing its scope summary.

Validation:
  - Require the deployment's administrative service identity and change record.
  - Verify UID exists in the configured Firebase project; resolve its server user.
    Reject unknown UID/project, expired grant, unknown capability or missing tenant.
  - Global registry capabilities use --global instead of --institution-id; mutually
    exclusive flags. Never infer a global scope from a missing tenant argument.
  - The capability list, expiry and independence checks are exactly 15.3/19.1.
  - Independent source authors and reviewers use different verified identities.
  - Write a scoped grant and audit event atomically; never change FARMER domain,
    app membership or a farmer staff role. Grant expiry is checked on every use.
  - Repeat exact active grant is an acknowledged no-op; changed scope is a new grant.
  - Print grant ID/scope/expiry only; no token, phone, DB credential or source bytes.

19.4 PRODUCTION TRANSACTION AND PUBLICATION CASES
-------------------------------------------------
Purpose: Specify race/failure behavior before production implementation.

Controlled reconciliation command:
  File: scripts/institution/reconcile_job.py. Deployment operations identity only;
  it has no public route and grants no membership/operator capability to the app.
  Run from backend/ with the baseline Python environment:
    python -m scripts.institution.reconcile_job --job-id <UUID>
      --operation-key <persisted-key> --provider-task-id <actual-id>
      --reason <change-record-id> --dry-run
  --confirm-not-accepted replaces --provider-task-id only when the provider can
  positively verify absence. Replace --dry-run with --apply after reviewing proof.
  - Fetch the provider record under its configured project, compare operation key,
    payload/input hash and resource scope; a caller-supplied task ID is not proof.
  - Require FAILED/EXTERNAL_OUTCOME_UNKNOWN, lock job and store a nonsecret
    stage_outputs.reconciliation record: {operation_key,resolution ACCEPTED or
    NOT_ACCEPTED,provider_task_id nullable,proof_hash,verified_at,system_principal,
    change_record}. Record an audit event in the same transaction.
  - ACCEPTED resumes/polls the verified original task; NOT_ACCEPTED permits its
    original submission step. Preserve input/actor/generation and recheck access.
  - Refuse unresolved proof, wrong project/hash, exhausted attempt or expired
    deadline; never reset counters/budget or invent an accepted provider result.
  - Exit nonzero on refusal; logs contain safe IDs/status only.

Receipt crypto adapter:
  File: services/shared/receipt_crypto_service.py.
    encrypt(plaintext_bytes, associated_data_bytes) -> {ciphertext,key_id}
    decrypt(ciphertext, key_id, associated_data_bytes) -> plaintext_bytes
  - The local AES-256-GCM adapter uses the private project key file;
    AAD binds schema version, full receipt scope, request hash and secret expiry.
  - Store returned ciphertext/key version only; no homegrown or plaintext fallback.
    Preserve the same private key for the receipt TTL and across deployments;
    missing original keys require authorized invitation reissue.
  - Missing/unavailable local receipt key blocks invitation create/reissue with safe 503 BEFORE
    changing rows; unrelated farmer/accounting services stay available.

Case                         | Required transaction result
-----------------------------|--------------------------------------------------
Concurrent same-key write     | One mutation/receipt; loser reads that receipt.
Expired original receipt     | No mutation; 409 with authorized recovery reference.
Competing audit revisions    | One working family row; other gets REVIEW_IN_PROGRESS.
Reviewer leaves membership   | Historical contribution still disqualifies self-review.
Control changes during claim | Close obsolete case; no stale control approval.
Draft edit during calculation | Old job cannot attach preview to the new input hash.
Provider accepted, reply lost | Reconcile persisted operation identity; never blind replay.
Revocation before publish    | BLOCKED_ACCESS; no new artifact/result exposure.
Demo reset during a request  | Generation check before replay and again at commit.

Atomic publication rules:
  - Reload tenant/grant and lock the exact source/family plus job immediately before
    updating pointers. Compare frozen input hash, expected lineage and generation.
  - A stale draft output may remain private historical processing evidence; mark
    the job CANCELLED/INPUT_SUPERSEDED and do not overwrite the current preview.
  - Export/advice publication also checks original snapshot visibility; a successful
    provider call never bypasses revocation or published-context requirements.
  - All descendants of a queued operation wait for its durable ACKED receipt/ID map.
    Resolve local IDs/evidence and canonicalize while PENDING; after SENDING, body,
    hash, key and intended revision are immutable.
  - Period timestamps use [local midnight(period_start), local midnight(day after
    period_end)) in the saved IANA timezone, converted to UTC. Do not add fixed 24h
    durations across daylight-saving changes. DATE activities stay inclusive dates.

================================================================================

SECTION 20: v4.0 — GROUNDED ADVISORY AND RESEARCH DESIGN
================================================================================

PIPELINE
--------

  - scoped question -> multi-topic classifier -> immutable institution context ->
    pgvector semantic + PostgreSQL lexical candidates -> versioned rank fusion (RRF)
    -> applicability/reranker -> bounded evidence -> structured generation ->
    numeric/citation/schema validation -> backend action ranking/history.
  - Topics
    ENERGY/TRANSPORT/WASTE/WATER/SOLAR/BIOGAS/COMPOSTING/TREES/CARBON_BALANCE/YEAR_COMPARISON/GENERAL_SUSTAINABILITY.
  - Broad priorities consider whole profile.
  - Institution facts fetched relationally with tenant auth, never
    global-vectorized.
  - Context includes periods/coverage, totals/shares, existing actions, tree
    baseline/uncertainty, history, known constraints and IDs/units.
  - Unknown roof/budget remains unknown.
  - Direct numeric questions return deterministic context values.
  - Sources approved with publisher/license/geography/effective dates/type/version;
    OCR/extraction quality/page/table units preserved, chunk sizes/overlap
    configurable.
  - Embedding changes create new versioned indexes; corpus publish is reviewed
    atomic manifest, old lineage remains for history.
  - Mandatory access/active-version filters never relax; broader geographic evidence
    can supplement with explicit limitation.
  - Classifiers/rerankers/thresholds configured/versioned and calibrated on
    validation; not fake credibility probabilities.
  - Cite real supplied passages and compatible historical/current applicability.
  - Conflicting evidence surfaces disagreement.
  - LLM input redacted and bounded, documents untrusted.
  - No SQL/write/minting tools.
  - Typed response separates DATA/DOCUMENT/ADVISORY.
  - Validate source IDs and claims, numerical facts, unsupported impact, schema;
    bounded retry then partial/failed.
  - Automatic support checks are imperfect: retain warnings and expert research
    review.

ACTION RANK
-----------

  - Initial RULES_V1 provides the ordinal ordering in 20.1.
  - Later numeric ranking requires independent approval of normalized components,
    weights, UNKNOWN-component policy, stable tie-break and config version.
  - LLM proposes/explains supported actions; backend ranks.
  - Existing action overlap, remaining opportunity/site constraints and missing data
    explicit.
  - High electricity share is not proof HVAC is the cause.
  - Scenario service uses explicit quantities/baseline/approved factors, stores
    hash/assumptions/units/ranges and cannot amend audit totals.
  - Payback needs real inputs.
  - History retains context/query/retrieval
    scores/chunks/corpus/model/ranking/prompt settings and validation flags,
    timing/token/cost.
  - Cache tenant+UID+revision+constraints+ query+all versions; never question-only
    cross-tenant reuse.
  - Regenerate creates new record.
  - LLM/provider failure returns retriable advisory error, not accounting failure.

  - Research implementation is separate from operational delivery.
  - SRS 33.1 is canonical benchmark/control plan: reviewed profile-query cases,
  - blind expert judgments,
  - held-out institutions/families,
  - paired relevant/irrelevant changes,
  - strong context-aware baselines and component ablations,
  - retrieval/grounding/action/prioritization/abstention/latency/cost metrics.
  - Pin code/corpus/splits/models/seeds, repeat runs and report paired/clustered
    uncertainty, agreement, error analysis and negative results.
  - No fabricated benchmark sizes/novelty/gains or mixing synthetic with observed
    data.

20.1 IMPLEMENTABLE ADVISORY CONFIGURATION AND OUTPUT
----------------------------------------------------
Initial production behavior:
  - Q&A is one question + immutable selected context, not a memory/chat-agent system.
  - Use approved corpus and deterministic relational fact IDs/values/units.
  - Direct numeric questions return the exact snapshot fact or missing-data answer.
  - No calculated context -> NO_CALCULATED_CONTEXT; no finalized default context ->
    NO_FINALIZED_CONTEXT.
  - Explicit calculated drafts are OWNER/MANAGER-only.
  - Publish requires original snapshot to be the approved final snapshot of that
    audit revision.
  - Finalizing a different calculation does not legalize old advice.
  - Regenerate calls AskRequest with new context, creating a new query record.
  - New provider work may fail while old authorized history remains available.

Constraints schema — all optional, unknown by default:
  budget {value Decimal,currency TEXT,source TEXT}|null.
  time_horizon_months INTEGER|null >0; preferences TEXT[]; exclusions TEXT[].
  site_facts AdvisorySiteFact[]; exact fields are in 18.6;
  guidance_as_of DATE (server current date if omitted); user notes <=2000 chars.
  - Constraints cannot override factor/model approval or create measured facts.
  - Do not claim eligibility for schemes without applicable source/date evidence.

Versioned baseline retrieval configuration — design defaults, not measured optima:
  chunk_size_tokens=800; overlap_tokens=120; retain page/section/table lineage.
  vector_candidates=20; lexical_candidates=20; RRF_k=60;
  rerank_candidates<=30; final_context_chunks<=6.
  RRF_score(chunk) = sum_over_available_rank_lists(1 / (60 + rank)), rank starts at 1.
  - Require selected ACTIVE corpus/license/visibility before either retrieval query.
  - Apply geography/date/topic applicability, rerank/deduplicate, then bounded context.
  - No mandatory visibility relaxation.
  - Broader geographic guidance is explicitly labeled; expired policies never become
    current eligibility advice.
  - Corpus, embedding dimension/provider and reranker versions are pinned together.
  - Numeric retrieval thresholds are optional until validated; do not invent a
    universal similarity-to-truth cutoff.
  - Empty/conflicting support -> abstain/bound.
  - Provider/model IDs, licenses, context limits, tokenizer and budgets must be
    commissioned and approved in SRS 36.4; no claim that a model is already
    installed.

Default recommendation ordering — RULES_V1:
  - Classify candidates DATA_REQUIRED, SUPPORTED_ACTION, SITE_CHECK_REQUIRED.
  - When inventory is incomplete, relevant DATA_REQUIRED actions precede interventions.
  - Otherwise SUPPORTED_ACTION precedes SITE_CHECK_REQUIRED;
    unsupported/inapplicable actions are omitted with reason, not disguised as
    feasible priorities.
  - Within a class: known relevant category emission share descending, then stable
    action_id ascending; unknown share sorts after known.
  - Display ordinal priority.
  - Action cannot be 'supported' without evidence, applicable prerequisites and no
    known contradictory site constraint or already-implemented duplicate.
  - No invented numeric impact/feasibility/confidence score in RULES_V1.
  - A later weighted ranking needs approved normalization, weights, missing-data
    treatment and sensitivity evaluation; it is a separate config version.

Typed answer sections:
  summary TEXT; facts {fact_id,value,unit,snapshot_hash}[];
  document_claims {text,citation_ids[]}[];
  recommendations {action_id,category,title,rationale,priority_class,priority,
    prerequisites[],missing_data[],overlap_refs[],citations[],scenario_id nullable,
    scenario_capability {action,formula_key,required_inputs JSON Schema}|null}[];
    scenario_capability is server-derived from the supported action catalog;
    an LLM cannot enable a formula or supply a numeric result through this field.
  assumptions[],missing_data[],warnings[]; supported=false if substantive evidence absent.
  Source {citation_id,document_id,chunk_id,document_version,title,publisher,
    source_url,page nullable,section nullable,excerpt,checksum,applicability}.
  - citation_id must resolve to supplied chunk/version, never a model-invented URL.
  - Licensed excerpts respect the approved document/source policy; raw documents
    need not be redistributed.
  - Source deactivation adds warning to historical advice.
  - Validate structured schema, fact values/units/hash, citations and claimed impacts.
  - Allow one bounded regeneration on validation failure; then PARTIAL or FAILED.
  - Never publish unsupported recommendations/savings after failed validation.

Provider boundary:
  - Send aggregate context with opaque fact IDs, institution type/coarse region and
    volunteered constraints.
  - Exclude phone/Firebase UID/contact, raw bills/photos, exact campus geometry and
    private file URLs.
  - Explicitly approved private context transmission requires recorded consent and
    provider retention policy; no silent raw-document upload.
  - Evidence text is untrusted: no instructions/tools/SQL/writes/minting privileges.
  - Scope cache by UID/tenant/snapshot/question/constraints/all model/corpus versions.
  - Cache visibility is revalidated each read; payload redaction precedes logging.
  - Store safe provider request IDs/tokens/latency/cost, never secret
    prompts/documents in routine logs.
  - Cost unknown remains unknown, not fabricated zero.

================================================================================

SECTION 21: v4.0 — SECURITY, CONFIGURATION AND OPERATIONS
================================================================================

  - FastAPI verifies Firebase token and all parent tenant relationships.
  - Supabase service credentials remain server-only; Firebase UID is not Supabase
    auth.uid().
  - Explicit backend access checks plus implemented DB/storage policy defense; don't
    assume Firebase-driven RLS.
  - Private upload/download scope/expiry/hash validation, MIME/size/batch/rate
    limits and safe parsers; allowlisted source URLs prevent SSRF.
  - External LLM sees minimal redacted context under approved provider retention;
    private bills/scans stay outside shared corpus/public IPFS.
  - No credentials/raw documents in logs; structured
    tenant/domain/job/revision/error/timing metadata.
  - Client offline encryption/OS key lifecycle and revocation cleanup are release
    gates.
  - Server revision/idempotency checks reject unsafe replay after revocation or
    edits.
  - Versioned backups/database/storage/manifests and restore drills; archive
    preserves finalized provenance.
  - Short-lived signed URLs have bounded post-revocation lifetime.

  - Exact keys/defaults/validation/processes are in 21.6. Reuse baseline secrets.
  - Missing configuration blocks affected stage with actionable status.
  - Advisor flag off leaves institution audits usable.
  - Institution flag off behavior is fixed in 15.4: permitted released reads remain;
    new operational work is blocked.
  - Monitor farmer/institution queue depth, errors/latency, imagery coverage, model
    quality and provider spend.
  - Benchmark real capacity/cost; no Rs 0/month promise.

21.1 SECURITY LIMITS AND OPERATIONAL DEFAULTS
----------------------------------------------
These values are planned initial controls, configurable through reviewed deployment
policy. They are not statements about the current app or measured provider capacity.

Transport and storage:
  - HTTPS/TLS only for production API/storage/provider calls; no cleartext fallback.
  - Private buckets deny anonymous/mobile SDK access.
  - Firebase users use FastAPI, never a Supabase service-role key or assumed
    auth.uid() membership policy.
  - Use a least-privilege backend DB/storage role, separate migrations/operator role
    and scoped worker credentials.
  - Audit any service-role bypass before release.
  - Download URL lifetime <=60 seconds; upload URL <=5 minutes.
  - Signed-link access may persist until expiry after revocation; if immediate
    revocation is required, use authenticated download proxy rather than a long URL.
  - Evidence accepts PDF, JPEG, PNG; CSV only for AUDIT import parents.
  - GeoJSON is BoundaryCreate JSON; MODEL-only artifact exception is in 18.11.
  - Initial limit 10 MiB per evidence file, CSV 5 MiB; JSON body <=1 MiB;
    measurement batch <=200 items.
  - Validate extension, magic bytes, MIME, byte size and SHA-256 on server actual
    bytes.
  - File-format/parser checks and timeout/memory limits precede READY.
  - Reject executable/polyglot/unsupported/encrypted-unreadable evidence, return
    REJECTED + safe reason; quarantine objects are never downloadable by members.
  - Use generated bucket keys tenant/parent/evidence_id; filename never chooses path.
  - Signed upload URLs target a quarantine/attempt key only, never a READY key.
    Complete/check streams and verifies the actual bytes, then writes those SAME
    checked bytes to a fresh server-only verified/<evidence-id>/<byte-hash> key.
    Verify the destination hash/size before atomically publishing READY + locator.
  - A still-valid upload URL can change only its quarantined object, never the
    promoted immutable object. No client PUT/update/upsert permission covers READY
    paths. Source changes during scan/copy fail/restart verification, not approval.
  - Crash recovery checks staged destination by ID/hash; publish once under the
    evidence/job lock and garbage-collect unreferenced staging only after policy.
  - Workers write report/map artifacts to private staging, verify the final object,
    then commit its immutable locator/byte hash/READY status. No in-place overwrite.
  - READY object bytes are immutable; replacement uses new evidence_id/version.
  - Garbage-collect expired unreferenced uploads after 24h; never delete referenced
    accepted evidence.
  - Upload/READY alone does not link evidence into a frozen audit.

Rate and work budgets:
  - Initial per-UID/tenant API reads 60/min, draft writes 30/min, advice asks
    10/hour.
  - Own registration/privacy/inbox limits use UID scope before tenant creation.
  - Initial active campus-satellite jobs <=1 per tenant; advice jobs <=2 per tenant.
  - Apply provider/project-wide quotas as well; do not assume all tenants get an
    independent GEE/LLM allowance.
  - Return 429 + Retry-After without duplicate jobs.
  - Dedicated institution raster/advisory queues cannot consume farmer mint/fusion
    worker slots.
  - Measure and reserve farmer throughput before enabling the feature.
  - Separate worker memory/time limits: initial raster deadline 6h,
    accounting/export 10min, upload check 5min, advisory 90s.
  - Provider deadlines fit inside these budgets.
  - The watchdog/retry policy in 19.2 handles deadlines.
  - These are maximum work budgets, not promised completion times or availability
    guarantees.
  - Redis limiter/lease failure blocks new protected mutations with 503; accepted
    DB/outbox work remains durable and recoverable.

Error codes and mobile recovery:
  401 TOKEN_EXPIRED/SESSION_REVOKED -> one Firebase refresh attempt; revoked/disabled
    identity -> sign out and destroy institution local keys. Do not loop refresh.
  403 MEMBERSHIP_REVOKED/PERMISSION_DENIED -> clear affected view; no retry writes.
  404 RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND -> safe missing/inaccessible message, no tenant details.
  409 AUDIT_REVISION_CONFLICT/MEASUREMENT_CONTENT_CONFLICT -> compare/reconcile.
  409 LAST_OWNER_REQUIRED -> transfer ownership first.
  422 INVALID_GEOMETRY/MISSING_FACTOR/UNIT_MISMATCH/OVERLAPPING_ACTIVITY/
    INCOMPATIBLE_BASELINE/INSUFFICIENT_SAMPLES -> correction task with field paths.
  503 METHOD_NOT_CONFIGURED/INSTITUTION_FLOW_DISABLED -> disabled affected action,
    retryable=false until operator configuration changes.
  503 SATELLITE_UNAVAILABLE/MODEL_PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE -> retryable only when
    genuinely transient; unavailable scientific coverage is NEEDS_DATA instead.
  - Access failure never leaves another tenant's stale content behind its banner.

21.2 OFFLINE AUTHORIZATION, ENCRYPTION AND SYNC
-----------------------------------------------
Storage:
  - Institution sensitive drafts/photos use a dedicated encrypted vault, not the
    current unencrypted terratrust-store MMKV instance.
  - AES-256-GCM authenticated encryption with unique nonce per write; wrapping/data
    key lifecycle protected by Android Keystore.
  - Never serialize keys into Redux.
  - Namespace by UID/domain/tenant/asset/revision; exclude vault/plaintext temp
    files from device backup.
  - Delete temporary decrypted media when its view closes.
  - Same-UID tenant switch can retain encrypted unsynced drafts but clears visible
    views/caches.
  - Reopening another tenant requires its fresh or unexpired context.
  - Logout default is Sync and log out / Discard and log out / Stay.
  - No retained institution-sensitive vault/key or exported plaintext after logout.
  - Known revocation -> stop sync, destroy that tenant key/cache/temp files, notify
    user that unsynced work cannot be submitted.
  - No export-after-revocation loophole.

Offline context:
  - Online download returns UID/tenant/permission, geometry/policy/version manifest,
    authorization_checked_at and offline_context_expires_at (initial max 24h).
  - Cached permission is a time-limited local fieldwork allowance, not server auth.
  - Expired context locks sensitive drafts until online revalidation; it does not
    silently discard saved work or fabricate a valid session.
  - Record monotonic elapsed time and last server time to detect backwards clock
    changes.
  - Invalid/reboot-ambiguous context requires online refresh.
  - After remote revocation unknown offline, bounded cached access may persist until
    expiry; disclose this limitation.
  - Online server still rejects every revoked write.
  - New offline tree/capture UUIDs belong only to the already authorized exact
    survey/campus/plot grant. A new survey requires online creation and preparation.
    Offline capture never grants review, network model work or final submission.

Queue record (same state machine as FDD 22.3):
  - Canonical mobile type: QueueOperation in contracts/local.ts (FDD 22.3).
  - States: PENDING -> SENDING -> ACKED; conflict -> CONFLICT; dependency/access/
    validation failure -> BLOCKED. A visible "queued" label means PENDING.
  - Persist original method/path/canonical body/hash/idempotency key/If-Match and
    baseInputRevision before SENDING. Include dependency IDs and verified byte IDs.
  - SENDING after restart means unknown outcome; replay its original request before
    retrying descendants or allowing discard. Never regenerate its key/body.
  - ACKED stores the validated receipt and returned resource/counter mapping.
  - CONFLICT/BLOCKED requires explicit reconciliation/revalidation. A changed body
    creates a new operation; no silent transition of an old request to new content.

Reconnect order:
  Step 1: Verify Firebase session and fresh membership/capabilities/asset revision.
  Step 2: Resolve local survey/tree creates and tag matches; maintain local->server ID map.
  Step 3: Upload/check evidence to READY, preserving byte hash and returned IDs.
  Step 4: Apply mutation at ORIGINAL edit revision or its own acknowledged chain;
          unrelated server changes pause for conflict reconciliation first.
  Step 5: Persist acknowledgement and new revision before removing local operation.
  Step 6: Refresh validation; submit only after all dependencies/READY evidence sync.

Conflict resolution:
  - Network uncertainty retries the SAME operation/key/hash, not a newly generated key.
  - Cross-device 409 keeps base/local/server views.
  - Show changed fields and offer Reload server / Keep local copy and reconcile /
    Stay.
  - User-confirmed merged category saves against the newly fetched input_revision
    with a NEW operation ID.
  - Never blindly retry full replacement over newer data.
  - A frozen/accepted revision needs explicit correction revision first; update
    queue/route IDs to the returned new revision.
  - Draft edits do not create random business revisions just to bypass conflict
    checks.
  - Duplicate stable tree tag requires matching the existing identity, not cloning.


Android adapter and recovery requirements:
  - InstitutionVault uses AES-256-GCM with unique nonce, UID/tenant/record AAD,
    per-namespace Android Keystore key, atomic encrypted files in noBackupFilesDir.
  - This includes confirmed profile/context, drafts, requests and selected bytes;
    institution data is not serialized into ordinary farmer MMKV/persisted Redux.
  - Confirmed same-UID cold offline restore is allowed only on a transport failure,
    same boot, unexpired <=24h server grant and continuous monotonic clock.
  - Explicit backend denial, new/pending demo login or generation mismatch cannot
    use it.
  - Cache assessment-config by survey+campus+boundary/mask/plot/policy version;
    another survey cannot replace its lineage. enabled_actions is server-owned.
  - Capture UID/tenant/generation/session epoch before picker/native/hash/lock
    waits; recheck before storage/network/state writes.
  - Old denial cannot clear new context.
  - Forms retain their ORIGINAL edit revision through refresh/local save.
  - Only acknowledged operations from this local dependency chain may advance the
    base; an unrelated server edit requires explicit reconciliation and new
    operation ID.
  - Unknown online outcomes retain encrypted exact body/key/revision in SENDING;
    resolve original receipt before discard.
  - Concurrent identical actions share it.
  - Sync-and-logout checks own registration plus ALL accessible tenant namespaces.
  - Incomplete saved forms need explicit save/discard; sync does not submit them.
  - Read-only/fresh accounts can sign out without write-capable sync.
  - Explicit discard-and-logout removes all local institution work, not accepted
    server rows.
  - Report download validates byte size/SHA-256; mobile limit 20 MiB.
  - FileProvider shares temporary decrypted content only on user action,
    revokes/removes it after five minutes or logout; user-saved exports follow the
    user's external storage.
  - Supported Android resource links: terratrust://institution/{kind}/{UUID},
    kind=audit|campus|survey|tree-assessment|advice. Reject query/fragment/token and
    extra segments. Fresh auth/resource/tenant validation precedes navigation;
    cross-tenant link requires explicit authorized switch. Invitations use paste.

21.3 PRIVACY, ACCOUNT DELETION AND RETENTION
--------------------------------------------
Product surfaces:
  - Institution Profile and restricted setup/status show Privacy policy, Support and
    Delete institution account.
  - They are available without active membership.
  - In-app request/track/cancel uses institution-account deletion routes.
  - Publish an external verified deletion-request page at the configured public
    account-deletion URL; it must work without reinstalling the app.
  - This addition applies to the institution flow.
  - Evaluate existing farmer release policy separately; this documentation task does
    not change that implemented flow.

Deletion workflow:
  - Require recent verified phone sign-in (initial auth_time age <=5min) for
    request, cancel and ownership-sensitive actions.
  - Reauthentication preserves flow intent.
  - REQUESTED -> NEEDS_ACTION when user is sole OWNER; show
    transfer/reviewer-assisted resolution, not an irreversible deletion that orphans
    accepted tenant records.
  - Scoped PRIVACY_OPERATIONS operator verifies requester, retention policy and
    ownership resolution, then APPROVED -> PROCESSING -> COMPLETED.
  - A user can CANCEL before PROCESSING. Processing stages are idempotent and audited.
  - Revoke memberships/tokens, delete allowed personal/local/provider data and
    handle Firebase identity under the approved policy.
  - Retained shared finalized evidence has its disclosed lawful/governance retention
    basis; redact/anonymize permitted PII.
  - Identity unlinking cannot corrupt immutable carbon values/hashes/chain of
    review.
  - Keep a nonidentifying audit actor reference where retention is justified.
  - Completion records deleted/retained categories and provider confirmations.
  - Operator workflow includes verified external-page requests in the same queue.
  - No arbitrary default number of legal retention years or deletion-completion promise.

Immutable-row and byte-purge implementation:
  - Delete or pseudonymize personal users fields under the approved plan; preserve
    the internal actor UUID needed by retained FKs. Remove Firebase UID/phone and
    revoke the external identity when policy requires; never recreate it on refresh.
  - Retain evidence/artifact metadata, source hashes and immutable numerical rows
    when the plan requires history; do not DELETE CASCADE those rows.
  - If policy requires deleting their bytes, set bytes_deleted_at and
    purge_policy_version through the narrow deletion worker, then confirm storage
    deletion with a safe receipt. These lifecycle fields are outside scientific hash.
  - bytes_available=false while purge is committed/pending deletion. Historical
    READY means previously verified content, not current permission to download.
    New attachments/publication require READY AND actual unpurged verified bytes.
  - Authorized download of purged bytes -> 410 RESOURCE_BYTES_PURGED, no signed URL.
    Unauthorized access remains protected 404. Never serve a replacement/fake file.
  - Results retain their original numeric/source hashes. New authorized reports add
    SOURCE_BYTES_UNAVAILABLE disclosure without altering numerical result_hash.
  - Apply the policy to source-derived text/chunks/advice/provider copies too;
    deactivate unavailable source content for new retrieval and disclose historical
    redaction. Numerical history is separate from permitted personal-text deletion.
  - Deletion stays PROCESSING/FAILED_RETRYABLE until required provider/storage
    receipts succeed. COMPLETED reports actual retained/deleted categories only.

External deletion page implementation:
  - At INSTITUTION_ACCOUNT_DELETION_URL show the same privacy/retention disclosure,
    phone verification, request/status/cancel and support controls without requiring
    the mobile app.
  - Use the same Firebase project/phone identity through an approved web-auth client
    and allowed host; no unauthenticated phone-number-only deletion.
  - After verified Firebase token, use /auth/me with INSTITUTION intent, then the
    SAME institution-account/deletion-requests APIs and Error/Success schemas.
  - Show domain mismatch/access/last-owner blockers; never provision a second UID.
  - Recent authentication and operator execution rules above apply.
  - Do not put Firebase/provider secrets in page source.
  - Request tokens stay out of URLs/logs.
  - This is a required commissioned page contract, not a page built by this edit.

Retention policy — required before public release:
  - Approved durations/bases for finalized records, drafts, source evidence, advice,
    logs, backups, rejected uploads and completed deletion requests.
  - Legal hold/access/expiry/redaction rules; who approves exceptions; deletion job.
  - Provider training/retention setting, processing geography and consent/disclosure.
  - Production feature enablement fails if privacy/support/deletion URLs or required
    retention policy/version/operator owner are missing.
  - Archive institution/campus through reviewed lifecycle, not DELETE CASCADE.
  - Account deletion, campus archive and tenant record retention are distinct
    actions.

21.4 DEPLOYMENT, RECOVERY AND MONITORING CONTRACT
------------------------------------------------
Deployment:
  - Commission actual FastAPI/backend repository first; no assumed deployed files.
  - Lock validated runtime/dependencies; CI checks contracts/migrations/security and
    existing farmer regression.
  - No forced mobile dependency upgrades.
  - Separate dev/staging/production Firebase projects, DB/storage/provider keys and
    fixture settings.
  - Deny production synthetic identities/data/reset endpoints.
  - API, dispatcher/watchdog and institution workers have explicit process/queue
    roles.
  - Never start long raster work inside a synchronous web request.
  - Migration deploy is additive/expand-first.
  - Existing farmer fields/contracts keep semantics; nullable/domain constraints are
    backfilled before enforcement.
  - Readiness checks DB/storage/queue/config; advisor failure degrades advice only.
  - Liveness checks process health, not a live GEE or LLM request on every probe.
  - Release institution flags for a restricted approved pilot after SRS 36.4 gates.

Backups and targets — planned initial acceptance targets:
  - RPO <=1h, RTO <=4h for accepted DB/evidence data; provision backup/PITR/object
    version/copy services that can meet this, rather than claiming free-tier
    support.
  - Restore a matched DB + private object/manifest checkpoint in isolated staging.
  - Reconcile hashes, pending jobs/outbox and audit/advisory references before
    serving.
  - Perform documented prelaunch restore and repeat after material storage/migration
    changes; preserve deletion/retention requests when restoring backups.
  - Planned load gate: ordinary authorized reads p95 <=2s, draft writes <=3s at 25
    concurrent institution users, with no regression beyond agreed farmer baseline.
  - Benchmark real imagery/provider queues and operating cost separately.
  - Job limits above are caps, not guarantees of instantaneous satellite/advisor
    results.

Observability and incident response:
  - Track request/job IDs, errors, queue/lease age, dispatch lag, retries, evidence
    readiness, revision conflicts, model coverage/validation and provider spend.
  - No raw tokens, private files, invitation links or unredacted questions in logs.
  - Alert on stuck accepted job >its configured deadline, outbox lag >5min,
    sustained error-rate/quota exhaustion or unauthorized-access spike; named
    on-call owner.
  - Disable new institution/advisor work by flag; keep permitted released reads.
  - Pause workers safely/checkpoint; rollback compatible app/API, not additive data
    by destructive rollback or deletion of accepted records.
  - Reconcile unfinished work before re-enable; record incident actions/results.

21.6 CONFIGURATION DICTIONARY AND PROCESS TOPOLOGY
--------------------------------------------------
These are exact institution extension keys; retain farmer baseline keys/settings.
No .env value is changed by this specification. Backend app/config.py validates
configuration at startup. Flags use literal true/false; IDs below are approved UUIDs.

Backend key                              | Type/default and use
-----------------------------------------|------------------------------------------
ENABLE_INSTITUTION_FLOW                   | bool=false; new business writes/jobs
ENABLE_INSTITUTION_ADVISOR                | bool=false; new ask/scenario work
ENABLE_INSTITUTION_DEMO_ACCOUNTS                          | independent institution bool=false; test only
ENVIRONMENT                              | Existing development|production setting; no duplicate deployment variable
Model engine                             | Shared services.fusion_engine.run_fusion; no model env input
Evidence bucket                          | Code constant institution-evidence
Report bucket                            | Code constant institution-reports
INSTITUTION_PRIVACY_POLICY_URL           | HTTPS; required before public enablement
INSTITUTION_SUPPORT_URL                  | HTTPS; required before public enablement
INSTITUTION_ACCOUNT_DELETION_URL          | HTTPS; verified external request page
LLM_PROVIDER                            | GEMINI; required if advisor on
LLM_BASE_URL                            | unused compatibility field; native Gemini endpoint
LLM_MODEL                               | pinned provider model ID; no default
GEMINI_API_KEY                          | server secret; required if advisor on
LLM_TIMEOUT_SECONDS                     | int=60, range 1..80
LLM_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS                    | int=2000; <=approved model context budget
EMBEDDING_PROVIDER                      | GEMINI; exact commissioned corpus model
EMBEDDING_BASE_URL                      | unused compatibility field
EMBEDDING_API_KEY                       | unused; Gemini uses GEMINI_API_KEY
EMBEDDING_MODEL                         | pinned model ID; no default
EMBEDDING_DIMENSION                     | int>0; must match published corpus/model schema
EMBEDDING_BATCH_SIZE                    | int=32; provider's validated maximum applies
Reranker version                        | Derived from actual corpus text/config hash
ACTION_RANKING_CONFIG_VERSION           | RULES_V1 initially; approved config ID
RAG_VECTOR_CANDIDATES                   | int=20, range 1..100
RAG_LEXICAL_CANDIDATES                  | int=20, range 1..100
RAG_RERANK_CANDIDATES                   | int=30, range 1..30
RAG_TOP_K                              | int=6, range 1..6
RAG_RRF_K                              | int=60
CHUNK_SIZE_TOKENS                      | int=800
CHUNK_OVERLAP_TOKENS                   | int=120; less than chunk size
INSTITUTION_API_READS_PER_MINUTE        | int=60 per UID/tenant
INSTITUTION_API_WRITES_PER_MINUTE       | int=30 per UID/tenant
INSTITUTION_ADVICE_PER_HOUR             | int=10 per UID/tenant
INSTITUTION_UPLOAD_MAX_BYTES            | int=10485760 (10 MiB)
INSTITUTION_CSV_MAX_BYTES               | int=5242880; max rows=10000
INSTITUTION_JSON_MAX_BYTES              | int=1048576
INSTITUTION_DOWNLOAD_MAX_BYTES          | int=20971520; generation also enforces cap
INSTITUTION_BATCH_MAX_ITEMS             | int=200
INSTITUTION_SIGNED_UPLOAD_TTL_SECONDS   | int=300; maximum 300
INSTITUTION_SIGNED_DOWNLOAD_TTL_SECONDS | int=60; maximum 60
INSTITUTION_OFFLINE_MAX_SECONDS         | int=86400; maximum 86400
INSTITUTION_JOB_MAX_RETRIES             | int=3; retries after initial attempt
INSTITUTION_RASTER_DEADLINE_SECONDS     | int=21600
INSTITUTION_ACCOUNTING_DEADLINE_SECONDS | int=600
INSTITUTION_EXPORT_DEADLINE_SECONDS     | int=600
INSTITUTION_UPLOAD_DEADLINE_SECONDS     | int=300
INSTITUTION_ADVISORY_DEADLINE_SECONDS   | int=90
INSTITUTION_INGEST_DEADLINE_SECONDS     | int=1800
INSTITUTION_RASTER_CONCURRENCY          | int=1 initially; separate worker pool
INSTITUTION_ADVISORY_CONCURRENCY        | int=1 initially; <=2 active jobs/tenant

Configuration resolution:
  - Existing Firebase project/Admin credentials, DATABASE_URL/Supabase credentials,
    GEE/Google service account and Redis/Celery settings use the baseline names in
    Section 1.11.
  - No second credential copy or institution-specific auth system.
  - Short prose names such as LLM_TIMEOUT/MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS, EMBEDDING_DIMENSION,
    CHUNK_SIZE/OVERLAP and RAG_CANDIDATE_K were conceptual groups; the exact keys
    above replace them.
  - No MIN_EVIDENCE_SCORE default is invented; optional later evidence thresholds
    belong to an approved corpus/retrieval config version.
  - Deployment-owned registry stores immutable policy/model/provider/license/host
    configuration plus hash under SRS 36.4.
  - Retention is policy JSON, not guessed legal durations.
  - Record its UUID and reviewed content with deployment artifacts.
  - Env IDs select NEW work defaults; accepted jobs always read their frozen version
    content.
  - Updating env does not alter historical snapshots or floating factors.
  - Validate flag dependency: advisor cannot accept new work unless flow is enabled.
  - Approved reads/status/privacy remain available while flags are off.
  - Disabling a feature rejects new acceptance.
  - Already accepted jobs may complete with valid access/version/generation; an
    explicit operations pause stops workers.
  - Revocation/suspension always blocks publication, regardless of flag state.
  - Missing enabled-stage config -> readiness failure for that feature + safe 503;
    farmer readiness is evaluated separately.
  - Provider outage degrades advisor only.
  - Receipt encryption uses local cryptography.AESGCM; the generated private
    .institution-private/receipt.key is required for invitation create/reissue.
    It is a server key identifier, never mobile configuration or a raw crypto key.

Mobile public keys:
  - Reuse existing API URL/Firebase/maps; configure the two demo flags independently.
  - Add only INSTITUTION_PRIVACY_POLICY_URL, INSTITUTION_SUPPORT_URL and
    INSTITUTION_ACCOUNT_DELETION_URL to public build typing/template when
    implemented.
    Backend /auth/me includes matching public institution_links {privacy,support,
    account_deletion}; differing configured hosts block sensitive link actions.
  - No LLM/embedding/DB/service-account/token/registry secret belongs in mobile env.
  - Native vault, capture/hash bridge and FileProvider require a native Android
    rebuild.
  - Baseline package versions remain; no blanket dependency upgrade.

Queue/process contract (names are internal, never farmer queue aliases):
  tt_institution_raster : campus_mask, campus_plot_generation, campus_fusion
  tt_institution_accounting : institution_calculation
  tt_institution_exports : report_export
  tt_institution_evidence : evidence_check
  tt_institution_advisory : advisory
  tt_institution_ingestion : knowledge_ingestion, embedding_reindex
  tt_institution_privacy : account_deletion
  - API process: uvicorn main:app --host 0.0.0.0 --port 8000.
  - Worker example: celery -A tasks.celery_app worker -Q tt_institution_raster
    --concurrency=1; start the other named pools with their reviewed budgets.
  - Dispatcher: python -m tasks.institution_dispatcher; reads pending DB outbox.
  - Watchdog: python -m tasks.institution_watchdog; polls every 30s, stage leases
    heartbeat every 15s, expire after 90s; provider polling checkpoints avoid
    holding a CPU lease for a 6-hour GEE task.
  - Reconcile provider ID before resubmission.
  - Do not replace farmer worker command/queue. Reserved farmer capacity is a gate.
  - New adapter dependencies: pgvector, a safe PDF extractor/renderer, HTTP client,
    provider client, local reranker/tokenizer; select exact compatible versions into
    requirements lock after adapter tests.
  - API request/config contracts above remain fixed.
  - Python xgboost is required only for the XGBOOST adapter.

21.7 PRODUCTION CONTRACT MIGRATION AND FAILURE GATES
----------------------------------------------------
Purpose: Apply the documented institution contracts without changing farmer data
or claiming that a document review is a deployment acceptance result.

Fresh build:
  - Follow 22.2 in numeric order. New institution rows meet the complete schemas
    immediately; 011 verifies/adds the constraints introduced by this review.
  - In production skip test-only 010 and continue to 011. Do not deploy test grants,
    demo bundles, UID allowlists or reset API handlers to production processes.
  - The database configuration and local receipt encryption are required before
    their capabilities are enabled; new core reads/calculations need no receipt encryption call.

Upgrade if institution migrations were already applied:
  1. Keep applied 001..010 files/checksums intact. Put new changes in 011, whose
     schema-version guard distinguishes existing columns from missing additions.
  2. Disable new institution writes and drain/pause institution queues at safe
     checkpoints. Preserve farmer API, worker capacity and existing final results.
  3. Rehearse against a matched staging backup. Preflight duplicate working-family
     rows, receipt-scope duplicates, orphaned parent/source references, unavailable
     raw captures and vector dimension/model conflicts. Report IDs/counts privately.
  4. Resolve conflicting working revisions through authorized operator return/
     correction. Migration refuses to choose a winner, auto-approve, drop a result
     or silently delete duplicate history. Current finals remain unchanged.
  5. Add nullable metadata/raw fields first. Backfill raw_input ONLY from the exact
     original retained write snapshot and verified MCJ1. Quantized NUMERIC values
     cannot reconstruct original decimal spelling; never invent matching raw JSON.
  6. Legacy raw_input may remain NULL as an explicit unavailable historical capture.
     New INSERT or observation edit requires a complete raw item/hash via a narrow
     validation trigger/service; correction cloning also requires original raw data.
     A direct legacy measurement read/edit without it returns 409
     RAW_CAPTURE_UNAVAILABLE with resurvey/correction guidance. Frozen historical
     result/report reads retain their existing accepted snapshot and honest lineage.
  7. Receipt rows without provable original scope become TOMBSTONE and cannot replay
     a secret or run a new mutation. Existing unencrypted invitation receipts must
     be encrypted under the commissioned key or compacted before enabling sharing.
  8. Install new partial unique/dimension/parent/metadata checks after validation.
     Use a bounded lock_timeout=5s for the migration transaction; failure rolls back
     and records no applied ledger row. Rehearse realistic table/lock duration.
  9. Verify physical schema, raw round trip, authorized replay/expiry, independent
     review/finalization and actual object availability. Reconcile provider jobs
     before resuming their original frozen identities/budgets.
 10. Record SRS 36.4/36.8 evidence, then enable a restricted institution pilot.

Failure gates:
  - Missing reviewed method/policy/model/source -> disable that action, safe 503 or
    NEEDS_DATA; no fallback scientific constants or farmer result dependency.
  - Duplicate family/unknown raw capture -> explicit correction blocker, not data
    overwrite. Missing local receipt key blocks invitation sharing only.
  - Unknown external outcome -> no repeated provider side effect until verified.
  - DB/broker/limiter failure cannot lose an already committed job/receipt.
  - Deploy/rollback never erases additive schema, retained proof or current finals.
  - Gate live acceptance on documented isolation, migration, restore, load, capacity,
    source/provider and Android checks. Keep status UNVERIFIED until evidence exists.

SECTION 22: v4.0 — MIGRATION AND VALIDATION GATES
================================================================================

1. Inspect actual deployed backend/schema/auth/model/roles/demo sessions
   first; backup and capture farmer contracts/known recent frontend behavior.

2. Add domain fields/defaults, institution tables/constraints/indexes/flags;
   keep farmer IDs/records/endpoints immutable. Add postgres vector extension
   only after available version/permissions/storage impact validated.

3. Introduce shared engine facade behind unchanged farmer adapter; do not
   mandate farmer algorithm/zone/allometry changes. Version any later method
   correction.

4. Implement institutional authority reviews/campus survey/satellite
   applicability, then factors/accounting/reports and separately
   advisor/corpus.

5. Register approved real source factors/methods/models; prepare real-service
   demo checkpoints. Synthetic formula cases remain separate arithmetic tests.

6. Verify formulas/units/overlap/baseline/noncomparability,
   tenant/role/ancestry/job/storage/cache isolation,
   revisions/supersession/last-owner concurrency, UUID/idempotency/import
   retries, offline conflicts and endpoint compatibility.

7. End-to-end campus AR+satellite -> baseline -> later comparable period ->
   partial scope inventory -> review/report -> grounded advice, with no farmer
   result or wallet/token dependency. Include missing
   sensors/species/uncertainty/provider failure and independent report retry.
   Run existing farmer regression/device evidence before enablement; no code
   tests are claimed by this document change.

8. Release by measured exit gates in SRS 36; rollback gates new institution
   work without deleting institutional or farmer accepted history. Research
   configs and results separate, evidence-based. No deployment/mainnet
   authorization here.

22.1 SHARED DEMO REGISTRY AND REAL-SERVICE RESTORE
--------------------------------------------------
Mode authorization:
  - ENABLE_INSTITUTION_DEMO_ACCOUNTS controls institution demos only; false by
    default. ENABLE_FARMER_DEMO_ACCOUNTS independently controls farmer demos.
  - Server configuration + verified test deployment + Firebase UID registry
    authorize reset.
  - Mobile mode/headers cannot grant server authority.
  - Legacy ENABLE_DEMO_ACCOUNTS is a farmer-only fallback; the explicit
    ENABLE_FARMER_DEMO_ACCOUNTS value wins. Institution demos do not depend on it.
  - Institution capability/model/source requirements and standard RBAC still apply.
  - Do not implement mock provider/result pipelines for institution demo accounts.

Checkpoint storage — private backend files, no demo database tables:
  .institution-private/demo/account-N.json holds each validated checkpoint and
  verified Firebase UID, with the actual source rows/private-object references.
  Real demo business data uses the same ordinary tables/providers as real users.
  Like farmer middleware, the API process stores a sign-in marker per UID.
  Use one local demo API process; reset replay uses an ordinary DB transaction.
  /auth/me resets accounts 5-8 once per new verified Firebase auth_time; refresh
  and resume preserve work. Account 9 never replays a checkpoint and remains
  persistent. No farmer middleware, checkpoint, identity or business data changes.
  Demo-session endpoints remain compatible; an extra handoff/generation header
  is optional. Server claims also reject stale logins. After reset, clear obsolete
  client queues; current workers reject previous-generation callbacks.
  Private checkpoint files must be backed up; demo markers clear on API restart.
  Migration 016 replaces the trigger scope guard and removes the historical
  demo_checkpoint_registry/demo_login_sessions control tables. Export their source
  manifests before applying it. Keep migration phases 001-015/checksums unchanged.

Restore and real services:
  - Stage rows/files, validate source lineage/checksums, atomically publish current
    READY generation.
  - Retry crash recovery with same session/generation.
  - Invalidate old jobs/uploads/exports/advice callbacks before restoring.
  - Actual provider tasks are cancelled when possible; obsolete callbacks stay
    private.
  - Scope reset by registered development tenant ownership, not data_origin alone.
  - Actual measured/provider LIVE data can exist in an authorized test tenant.
  - Reset discovers new dedicated demo institutions by created_by ownership;
    every existing member must be the configured demo user.
  - Account 5 starts with no tenant; later registration/work becomes owned reset
    scope.
  - Never enroll arbitrary request-supplied existing IDs.
  - Allow checkpoint restoration of that tenant's accepted/finalized development
    rows; production immutable-history rules remain enforced everywhere else.
  - Never modify original historical source, global factors/models/methods/corpus,
    Firebase identity, non-demo members or production/other-tenant records.
  - Normal core modules perform every new AR/upload/satellite/calculation/review/
    export/advisor action.
  - Restore saved output; do not replay historical provider calls or fabricate
    alternative results whenever a user logs in.
  - Missing checkpoint/source/provider config returns NOT READY/unavailable; no
    hardcoded carbon total, citation, PDF or successful job fallback.
  - Institution sign-in markers use API memory; farmer tracking is unchanged.
  - This section scopes development reset exceptions; it does not authorize deletion
    through production review/privacy routes or alter production farmer workflows.
